The controlled report is the file EQMS stored for this run..." at the top of its Report information sheet; Run Details shows the same statement above the files whenever a file is not a PDF. The wording says "the file EQMS stored", not "the PDF", so it stays true for a template that allows only CSV/XLSX. **A CSV is not changed:** it is a data-exchange file and a notice row would break the import of its data; its protection is (2). **(2) Verify File.** The Action menu of a file of a completed run has "Verify File": the person chooses the copy they hold; the server reads it only to calculate its SHA-256 (not stored) and answers whether it is byte for byte the stored file, with both hashes and sizes. It takes the permission, the object scope and the current-scope re-check of a download (it discloses nothing a download would not), and an expired or removed file answers that it can no longer be checked. Each check is recorded in the Audit Trail (`REPORT_ARTIFACT_VERIFIED`, and `REPORT_ARTIFACT_VERIFY_MISMATCH` for a file that does not match) and in the run timeline (`FILE_VERIFIED`, `FILE_VERIFY_MISMATCH`, reason `REPORT_FILE_MODIFIED`). **Limits, stated plainly:** a match proves the file is unchanged; a mismatch proves it is not the stored file, but not who changed it, and a file that was never made by EQMS cannot be traced to a run. The check does not stop anyone from editing the copy. Mailed copies cannot be recalled. **PENDING_DECISION (owner/QA):** which output format is the official one for each report type; whether CSV/XLSX may be produced at all for the report types about people and signatures; whether a mismatch should notify QA.


**Owner answers to REPORT-FILE-INTEGRITY-2026-11-16 (2026-11-16).** (1) **The official format of a report is always PDF**; CSV and XLSX are data copies. (2) **CSV/XLSX may not be produced for the report types about people and signatures** (enforcement pending: the owner must name the report sources that count). (3) **A file that does not match is not reported to QA**: it is recorded in the Audit Trail and in the run timeline only. **Open conflict:** a number of templates allow only CSV (for example RPT.0005, RPT.0006, RPT.0026 to RPT.0035) so no PDF exists for them; "always PDF" needs either a PDF format added to them or an exemption. The wording of the data-copy notice ("the controlled report is the file EQMS stored") stays until this is answered.


### REPORT-RUN-AUDIT-2026-11-16 — Report Run Details has an Audit Trail tab in place of Timeline (owner decision: "giữ Notices Sent riêng, làm đi"; status: built, not committed; QA to confirm)
**Decision.** The Timeline tab of Report Run Details is replaced by the Audit Trail tab used by every other detail screen (`AuditTrailTab`, entity `REPORT_RUN`, with the entries of the files of the run). **Notices Sent stays its own tab**: it names the recipients, which only the people who manage scheduled reports may see, and one notice per recipient and channel does not belong in the audit record of the run. **Access:** whoever may open the run (own runs, or all runs with `reports.run.view_all`) may read its Audit Trail; no separate audit permission (the rule of 2026-11-07 for tabs that are part of a screen). **What the tab holds:** the entries of the run (`REPORT_RUN_QUEUED`, `REPORT_RUN_RETRY_REQUESTED`), of its files (download, preview, Verify File, legal hold) and, new, what the system did to the run: `REPORT_RUN_COMPLETED` (file name, format, size, SHA-256), `REPORT_RUN_RETRY_SCHEDULED` and `REPORT_RUN_FAILED` (attempt, reason code, text, and whether another attempt follows). These are written with the actor "System" (no user account) in the same transaction as the change they record. **Accepted loss:** a run made before this change has no completed/failed entry in the Audit Trail, because none was written then and history is not rewritten; the operational rows in `report_run_events` stay in the database but are no longer shown on the screen (the owner did not ask to keep them for old runs; the default taken here is to accept the loss). **Open:** whether the entries of the system actor should be reviewed in an Audit Trail Review campaign like those of a person.


**Owner answer to REPORT-RUN-AUDIT-2026-11-16 (2026-11-16): the entries of the System actor are reviewed in Audit Trail Review like those of a person.** Source-confirmed: a review campaign takes every audit entry of its period with no filter on the actor (`AuditTrailReviewService.createCampaign`), so these entries are already in a campaign; they show as user "System" with no employee code. No code change was needed.

### REPORT-MODULE-NAMES-2026-11-16 — the module in a report of the Audit Trail is named as the sidebar names it (owner: "tôi tưởng tên module sẽ khớp tên ở sidebar"; status: built, not committed)
The Module column, the Module picker and the Module filter of the Audit Trail report type used the stored entity type (`ACCESS_PROFILE`, `TRAINING_ACTIVITY`, `Controlled Copy`, `ControlledCopyRecord`...). They now use the menu module of the Audit Trail screen (`AuditMenuModule`: "Document Control", "Training Management", "Security & Authorization", ...), from the same one list: `AuditMenuModule.sqlCase` builds the SQL from it, so the report and the screen cannot differ. The picker lists one entry per module (many entity types map to one module). A template or schedule saved with a stored entity type as its Module filter still selects the same records. The stored value stays the machine value; it is never used to show or to authorize. **Not placed yet (shown as "Other", not guessed):** `EMAIL_DESIGN`, `IN_APP_DESIGN`, `TABLE_EXPORT`; the owner is to say under which module they belong. **Not changed, same kind of problem:** the Action picker and column still show the stored action code (`REPORT_RUN_QUEUED`), and the "Entity Type" field of the Authentication Events report shows the stored value.


**Owner answers to REPORT-MODULE-NAMES-2026-11-16 (2026-11-16).** (a) `EMAIL_DESIGN` and `IN_APP_DESIGN` belong to Notification Administration, a menu of System Administration: they are shown under **System Administration**. (The e-mail templates `EMAIL_TEMPLATE` stay under Application Settings as before; the owner did not ask to move them.) (b) **`TABLE_EXPORT` is not placed: the owner does not know**; it stays "Other" until someone says. (c) **The Action and the Entity Type are shown in words too** (REPORT-ACTION-LABEL-2026-11-16): the Action of the Audit Trail report as the Audit Trail screen writes it ("Training Activity Created", acronyms in capitals, "Publish to Effective"), the Entity Type of the Authentication Events report in Title Case. Only the display changes: the Action picker still lists and filters by the stored code, so a saved template or schedule is not affected. The Backup Activity report already wrote its Action in words.


### REPORT-MODULE-FIXED-2026-11-17 — the module of an audit record is fixed by the system and is the TOP menu of the screen of the object (owner decisions: "đồng ý, làm đi" and "chức năng ở menu nào thì lấy theo menu cấp 1, cao nhất của nó"; status: built, not committed; QA to confirm)
**Rules.** (1) **The names are fixed by the system**: they do not follow the labels an administrator gives the menus (Configuration > Navigation Labels). The module is computed when the record is read and is not stored, so a rule that changed with a setting would rename old records; the audit record must read the same tomorrow as today. Only a change of this list in the code changes it, and that is released like any change. (2) **The module is the level-1 (top) menu of the screen where the object is shown or administered.** This replaces the earlier note in `AuditMenuModule` ("the grouping follows the area an object belongs to, not the screen that administers it"). Consequence: what is administered under System Administration reads "System Administration" even when it concerns another area: the policies and publishing templates of Document Administration, the knowledge categories, the training vendors, code rules, properties, work positions and notifications of Training Administration, the report properties, appearance, types and name formats of Report Administration, the backup policy, storage, alerts and disaster recovery of Backup & Restore Administration, the notification policy, e-mail templates and designs, the configuration, network and storage policies, and the dashboard layouts of the roles. What a person works with in a module menu stays in it (Document Control: documents, revisions, copies, records; Training Management: activities, matrix, assessments, job assignments, reviews, records; Reports: runs, files, schedules, templates; Security & Authorization: profiles, permission sets, rules, SoD, workflow roles and policies, sessions; Backup & Restore: backups, restores, scrub). (3) **A new entity type must be placed**: `AuditedEntityTypesHaveAModuleTest` reads the audit calls in the sources and fails the build when one writes a type that is not placed. **Still "Other":** `TABLE_EXPORT` (the owner does not know its screen) and the legacy `PROMPT_SPECIFICATION`, `PROMPT_GENERATION_RUN`; the test lists them and fails when one is placed, so the list can only shrink. **Placed by trace to the code and the navigation; left where they were because their screen could not be traced (QA to confirm):** `FORM_SETTINGS`, `OFFLINE_MEDIA`, `TRAINING_CALENDAR`, `TRAINING_INITIAL_PART`, `TRAINING_PERSON_POSITION`, `TRAINING_FORM_TEMPLATE`, `BACKUP_EXPORT`, `BACKUP_INSPECTION`. **Open:** the same top-menu rule for the screen filters of the Audit Trail itself, if its Module picker still lists stored values.


### REPORT-AUDIT-DISPLAY-2026-11-17 — Object Type, Object Code and Action of the Audit Trail are worded by fixed rules, so a record can be read and found (owner request: "xây dựng 1 biện pháp cụ thể… nhiều thành phần không có tên cụ thể hoặc không có mã cụ thể… Action đơn giản hoá, thông tin cụ thể xem ở Detail"; status: built, not committed; QA to confirm)
**Finding (source-confirmed on the DEV log of 41,899 records).** Many objects had no usable identity: a generic name ("Authentication Session", "System Configuration"), the type itself as the name (`TRAINING_ASSESSMENT`, `TRAINING_FORM_TEMPLATE`, `REPORT_PROPERTIES`), an identifier (UUID in 100% of `BACKUP_ALERT`, 51% of `REPORT_SCHEDULE`, a storage address in `STORAGE_OBJECT`), or nothing (`PUBLISHED_PDF`); one name for many objects (9,805 notifications under 51 event names). The Action showed hundreds of codes (449 in use). The search matched the raw codes only, not the words of the columns.
**Measure (one code path for the screen, the detail, the report and the search).** (1) **`AuditObjectCatalog`**: the Object Type is one singular name per kind whatever its spelling ("Controlled Copy" for three spellings, "Login Session", "Report File"); the Object Code is taken in a fixed order, the business number (document number, revision) first, then the entity code, then the name, then, for the types whose only identity is in the text of the record, from that text (the number and version of a training assessment or form template; the person a notification went to). It is cleaned by fixed rules: a UUID is cut out, an address keeps the name of its file, a value that only repeats the type or is a generic word is not an identity. A record left with no identity shows "-" (its type, action, user and date still find it); **nothing is guessed and nothing is invented**. (2) **`AuditActionVerb`**: the Action is one of 39 verbs (Create, Update, Delete, View, Submit, Review, Approve, Reject, Confirm, Cancel, Obsolete, Publish, Enable, Disable, Send, Send Failed, Login, Login Failed, Verify Failed, Access Denied, ...), decided by one ordered rule table (the verb ends the code, else starts it, else is anywhere; what follows FOR or TO is the object, not the verb). The same table is an SQL expression for the report, so the two cannot differ. A code no rule names keeps its own words; on the DEV log every one of the 449 codes is named. The full action, the description and the changes are on the detail screen; the stored codes are unchanged. (3) **Search** now also matches the number of the object (entity code, document number) and the words of the columns as shown: the type ("Report File"), the module ("Document Control") and the verb ("Approve"). (4) **The Action filter** lists the 39 verbs; a verb selects every action code it names (the older filter words still work for a saved link). (5) **The report type "Audit Trail"**: its Action column is the verb and the former wording is the field "Action Detail".
**Evidence.** The SQL form of the verbs equals the Java rule on all 449 action codes of the DEV log; the Object Code rules were run on 15,428 distinct (type, name) records of the DEV log and read by hand; unit tests `AuditActionVerbTest`, `AuditObjectCatalogTest`; the list of verbs on the screen equals the server list (a test fails when they differ). **Limits, stated plainly:** (a) an object with no name and no number in the record (a Login Session, the System Configuration, the singleton settings, a schedule or an alert recorded under its identifier by an older version) shows "-": the display cannot create an identity the record does not hold; (b) **PENDING_DECISION (owner): give each of these a name at the moment it is written** (a schedule by its name, an alert by its title, a session by user and device); this changes what is written to the audit log from now on and does not rewrite old records; (c) the Action is shorter on purpose: "Update" no longer says what was updated (see the changes on the detail screen); (d) not yet seen on the screen by a signed-in person.


### NOTIFICATION-ACCOUNT-EMAILS-2026-11-17 — the account e-mails that carry a secret are registry events that are e-mail only and always sent (owner decision 2026-11-17; status: BUILT, tests pass, deployed to DEV for check; not committed)
**Finding (source-confirmed).** Four account e-mails were sent by code outside the registry with a fixed e-mail message name: "MFA OTP Login Code" and "Password Reset Link" (AuthService), "Account Unlocked - Temporary Password" and "Multifactor Authentication No Longer Required" (UserManagementService). The first three were editable only as e-mail messages (listed as e-mail only in the Notifications Administration, `EMAIL_ONLY_TYPES`) and had no delivery policy; **the fourth had no e-mail message at all** (no migration seeds it), so `EmailService.sendEmail` logged an error and the e-mail was never sent. `MfaOtpEmailDispatchService` and `AsyncEmailDispatchService` have no publisher (dead listeners, left as they are).
**Decision.** (1) The four are registered as events `security.mfa_login_code`, `security.password_reset_link`, `security.account_unlocked`, `security.mfa_requirement_removed` (constants in `NotificationMandatoryEvents`). (2) **E-mail only:** their supported channel is EMAIL; they have no in-app wording, and the dispatcher never records an in-app notification for them, because the code, the reset link and the temporary password would otherwise be kept in the database and shown in the bell. (3) **Always sent (a mandatory kind):** the dispatcher sends them whatever the saved policy says (a policy that is off, on another channel, in a digest or in quiet hours is ignored), at once; the administration refuses to switch them off, to give them another channel, a digest, quiet hours or escalation (HTTP 400 with the reason). An administrator can still change the wording and who gets them. **This is an exception to D6 ("no notification is locked") and to the statement that there is no mandatory kind: it applies to these four events only.** (4) They go through the encrypted outbox (sealed per recipient, polled every second). (5) The maintenance notice to the administrator address (`MaintenanceAdminAlerts`) is **not** moved: the e-mail outbox does not run during maintenance, which is why that notice is sent directly and its in-app part is the in-app-only event `backup.maintenance_drained`. Its wording stays in code until the owner decides how to make it editable without the outbox (open).
**Wording.** Each event starts from the wording of the e-mail message it replaces; the e-mail messages stay for older callers.
**Evidence.** `NotificationMandatoryEventsTest` (5: the four codes; a request to switch off, hold back, escalate or move is refused and nothing is saved; recipients stay editable; an old policy that is off, in a digest, in quiet hours and on in-app still sends the e-mail at once; no in-app notification is recorded), `EveryRegisteredNotificationHasASenderTest`, `NotificationPlaceholderContractTest`, `NotificationPolicyDeliveryTest`, `NotificationAdministrationServiceTest`, the dispatcher tests (97 tests of the notification and user-management groups, 0 failures). The test found and fixed one defect before release: activating a policy asked for an in-app title, which an e-mail-only event does not have, so its recipients could not have been edited. **Open:** the screen does not yet show these events as locked (the server refuses a change; the switch is still drawn); the maintenance notice (above).

## AUDIT-OBJECT-REGISTRY (2026-11-17) — Object Type, Object Code and Action of an audit record
**Owner decisions (2026-11-17).** (1) **Object Type** = the default name of the function menu where the object is shown or administered, fixed by the system (does not follow Navigation Labels). A child object (batch, file of a run) uses the **parent menu name only**; it is found and exported by its own code in Object Code (e.g. CCB.SOP.10109.B001), and the audit trail of every code inside a batch is still written. (2) **Object Code** = a real business code, never a technical name; where no code is needed the real name stays. (3) **Action** = one short verb. (4) **D3 codes:** Job Assignments, Training Reviews, Specimen Signatures = employee code; Legacy Training Evidence = Training Number + Document Number; Training Record Exports = employee code or Department; Restore Backup = Restore Number; new numbers (field in FE, BE, API, DB and an Admin numbering setup like Document Control / Training): Scheduled Report Number, Audit Trail Campaign Number, Access Review Campaign Number, Object Access Rule Number, Backup Alert Number, Announcement Number. (5) **D4:** System Announcements get an Announcement Number. (6) **D5:** TABLE_EXPORT takes the screen the export was made from. (7) **D6:** old records get numbers by one audited backfill. (8) **D7:** the SETTINGS catch-all is split: module Application Settings, Object Type = the sub-menu (Business Units, Departments, Positions, Document Types...), Object Code = the real name (e.g. Quality Control). (9) **D8:** a type belongs to the module and menu where it is done. (10) The additional numbers need an Admin function to set their format, like the numbers of Document Control and Training; further numbers are proposed to the owner as a tick list (`AUDIT_OBJECT_NUMBERING_TICKLIST_2026_11_17.md`).
**Status.** Decisions recorded; no code changed. Table of all actions: `AUDIT_ACTION_TABLE_V2_2026_11_17.xlsx`.
**Implementation, 2026-11-17 (same entry).** Built on DEV, not committed; see `validation/AUDIT_OBJECT_REGISTRY_2026_11_17_CHANGE_RECORD.md`. (a) `AuditObjectRegistry` (generated from `AUDIT_ACTION_TABLE_V2_2026_11_17.xlsx`) gives every kind its default menu name; a test fails for a kind the code writes and the registry lacks. (b) Object Numbering (V798, `ObjectNumberService`, System Administration screen) with Scheduled Report, Audit Trail Campaign, Access Review Campaign, Object Access Rule, Announcement and Backup Alert numbers; the Restore Number already existed in the Backup Agent and is used. Existing records were numbered at start-up with one System audit entry per kind. (c) The `SETTINGS` catch-all is split per menu; records written before are read by their action. (d) Writers corrected so Object Code is a real code. (e) V799: permission set names are unique (all other configuration kinds already were).
**Open after the build:** the Restore request entry still names the backup (the Restore Number is made later by the agent); old TABLE_EXPORT rows stay in "Other"; menu names of the training kinds that did not run on DEV are proposed; no screen was checked with a signed-in user.

## AUDIT-DETAIL (2026-11-17) — what the Audit Trail Detail says about a change
**Owner request (2026-11-17):** the Detail of an audit record must describe the changes clearly: the four words of the list, internal fields out of the way, long settings readable, what happened to each value.
**Built (frontend):** a Record block (Module, Object Type, Object Code, Action) with a link to the whole history of the object (`entityId` filter of the list, from 2000 to now); file internals (checksum, storage keys, paths, scan results) moved to "Technical details"; a JSON setting or a list split into the values that really changed; each row marked Set / Changed / Cleared; a value saved as it was is not shown; the two codes under which one legacy-import event was written have one name.
**Built (backend):** the Detail shows the system's own sentence only when the entry has no change list, so a reason or note the person typed was invisible next to a status change. Every writer that had only a sentence now also passes its data as changes (`AuditChanges`, `TrainingStore.change`); the sentence (the evidence already kept) is unchanged. Not changed: entries about viewing, printing, exporting, downloading or a denied access (nothing changed), and status-only transitions (the Status row is the change).
**Decided by the owner (2026-11-17): one audit entry per event.** The legacy import wrote two audit rows for one event (`LEGACY_IMPORT` through the revision history and `DOCUMENT_LEGACY_IMPORTED` written separately, the same for effective and superseded). Both were signed; only the second carried the typed reason. Now only the entry of the revision history is written; the reason the person signed with is part of its comment, so the history of the revision and the Audit Trail are one record. It is found in the main Audit Trail, in the Audit Trail tab of the Revision (by its id) and of the Document (by its document number, stored on the row). Old rows are not rewritten; the screens give both old codes one name.
**Status:** built on DEV, not committed. Record: `validation/AUDIT_DETAIL_2026_11_17_CHANGE_RECORD.md`.

## LEGACY-IMPORT-DEPARTMENT-SCOPE (2026-11-17) — D5 amended: the Document Control Officer imports into any department
**Owner decision (2026-11-17), while testing a legacy import on DEV:** a Document Control Officer sees all the records of Document Control, so they must also be able to import a paper document into any department. D5 of the paper-training decision (a person imports only into their own department unless they hold `training.scope.all`) is amended.
**Rule now:** `DocumentService.requireLegacyDepartmentScope` lets through the holder of `training.scope.all` **or** of `documents.document.view_all` (the permission that gives the scope of all documents); everyone else still imports only into the department they belong to (matched by name). The permission `documents.legacy_import.manage` is still needed to import at all.
**Not changed:** the paper-training evidence still needs `training.activity.organize` and `training.approval.designate`; the Training screens keep their own department scope. No permission was given to any account.
**Status:** built on DEV, not committed; no unit test of this check exists (DocumentService is built with many collaborators); it is exercised by the DEV import run recorded with the audit-detail change.

**AUDIT-DETAIL addendum (2026-11-17): the Audit Trail tab of a Document includes the entries of its revisions.** Owner rule: one source of data shown in the main Audit Trail, in the tab of the Revision and in the tab of the Document. `AuditTrailService.getEntityPage` with `includeChildren` for a Document now adds the entries of those revisions of the document whose own Audit Trail tab the person may open (`requireScopedEntityView("Revision", …)`); a revision the person cannot see stays out. The Document screen asks with `includeChildren`. Verified on DEV with the legacy import of INS.9701 (see the change record).

## LEGACY-IMPORT-ONE-PERMISSION (2026-11-17) — Legacy Import has no permission of its own
**Owner decision (2026-11-17):** merge Legacy Import with the ordinary permission, so that creating a document is **one** permission.
**Rule now:** `documents.legacy_import.manage` is retired (migration `V801`, same pattern as V411 and V486). Importing a document that already exists outside the system (the document number check, the document with its own number, the revision chain, the paper-training number info) needs what creating any document needs: `documents.document.create`, or `documents.workspace.manage` which the create check already accepts (`DocumentAuthorizationService.canCreateDocument`). The e-signature, the justification and the department scope of the import are unchanged.
**Effect, as read from source:** nobody who could finish an import before loses the ability, because the import already ran the create check. **New:** every holder of the create permission (for example an Author) can now import with its own document number and a historical revision chain. The segregation reason given in V463 (a batch import has a larger blast radius than one revision) is no longer a reason to grant the two apart; it is accepted by this decision. The department scope still limits an Author to their own department unless they see all documents.
**To check by QA:** the two Access Profiles named in V367 (System Administration, Document Security Administrator) have the create permission removed on purpose, so they cannot import either; this was already true of the document step and now also of the revision step.
**Status:** built on DEV code, not committed; V801 not yet applied to any database. The Access Profile screen no longer lists Legacy Import.

## ACCOUNT-RESET-PASSWORD-EMAIL (2026-11-17) — Reset Password e-mails the temporary password
**Owner request (2026-11-17):** the e-mail after an administrator resets a password did not carry the new password; it must.
**Decided with the owner (option chosen from three):** a new mandatory, e-mail-only event `security.account_password_reset` ("Password Reset - Temporary Password"), built like `security.account_unlocked`: user name, temporary password and the sign-in address. It is sent only by `UserManagementService.resetPassword`. The general notice `security.password_changed` stays as it was and still carries no password, because it is also sent after a person changed their own password (`AuthService`) and would mail back a password they chose.
**Safeguards that were already there and still hold:** the person must set a new password at the first sign-in (`mustChangePassword`); every session of the person is revoked; the event cannot be switched off, moved to another channel or held in a digest; the password is never kept as an in-app notification; the e-mail is stored sealed in the outbox (`encryption_version`). The administrator still sees the password on the Reset Password screen.
**Open for QA:** a password sent by e-mail is readable by whoever reads that mailbox; the control is the forced change at first sign-in. The server code, not the screen, refuses a reset of the person's own account (`SELF_RESET_PASSWORD_MESSAGE`).
**Status:** built, not committed. Tests: `NotificationMandatoryEventsTest` (five mandatory events), `UserManagementServiceCapabilitiesTest` (the event is sent with the temporary password and the general notice carries none).

## CONFIGURATION-PAGES-SPLIT (2026-11-17) — each Configuration tab is its own menu page
**Owner request (2026-11-17):** the eight tabs of System Administration > Configuration become their own menu entries, with their own page title, breadcrumb, address and route, in the manner of Dictionaries.
**Built:** Configuration is a menu group (System Administration > Configuration) with eight pages, in the order the tabs had: General, Navigation Labels, Security, Notifications, Integrations, Preview File, Image Watermark, Features, at `/settings/configuration/<page>`. The title and the last breadcrumb are the menu text. The bare address `/settings/configuration` and an old `?tab=` link lead to the page they meant (the first page when the tab is missing); stored notifications with the bare address keep working. Each page loads the saved configuration, shows only its own settings, and Save/Reset send only the section that the page changed. The sidebar asks before it leaves a page with edits that were not saved, and a closed tab or a reload asks too (before this, tab changes kept an edit and leaving the screen dropped it without a question).
**Not changed:** permissions (`settings.configuration.view` shows every page, `.manage` shows Save, Reset and the panels that need it), the API (`/settings/system`), the stored configuration, the audit of a save.
**Open for the owner:** (1) the pages use the old tab texts, so "Notifications" and "Security" now also exist under Configuration beside the inbox "Notifications" and the group "Security & Authorization"; rename if that confuses. (2) A favourite set on the old Configuration entry (id `config`) now points at the group, not at a page. (3) Each page reads the whole configuration (logos and backgrounds make it over half a megabyte) where the screen read it once. (4) Notification events that carry the bare address (retention blocked, e-signature settings, configuration changed) still go through the redirect to General. (5) A permission per page was not asked for and was not added.
**Status:** built on DEV, not committed. Record: `validation/CONFIGURATION_PAGES_SPLIT_2026_11_17_CHANGE_RECORD.md`.

**CONFIGURATION-PAGES-SPLIT addendum (2026-11-17): the Audit Trail follows the pages.** Owner request: since the menu changed, check and update what the Audit Trail takes from it. The Object Type of an audit record is the default name of the menu where the object is administered (AUDIT-OBJECT-REGISTRY), and each page of Configuration is now its own menu, so a configuration save is written under the page of the settings it changed. **Built:** `ConfigurationAuditPage` gives the page of a setting by its section and path (Security, Notifications, Integrations, Features, Documents = Preview File; the General section is shared by General, Navigation Labels, Image Watermark and the OnlyOffice connection = Integrations / viewer = Preview File); `SystemConfigurationService` writes one audit record for each page that had a change (entity types `SYSTEM_CONFIGURATION_<PAGE>`, Object Code still "System Configuration", module System Administration), and one record under the old kind when nothing differs; the register names the pages, and maps the network policy to Security, the dashboard role layout to General and the storage usage policy to Integrations (so old records of those read by their page too); the notification event `system.configuration_changed` follows every page and links to General; the storage warning links to Integrations. **Unchanged on purpose:** records written before the split keep the entity type `SYSTEM_CONFIGURATION` and read "Configuration" (all pages together, they cannot be told apart); the Document Workflow Settings record also uses that old kind. **Open:** a save that changes two pages (only by the API, the screens save one page) gives two records for one request; "Notifications" and "Security" are now also Object Types of configuration records beside the inbox "Notifications" and the module "Security & Authorization" (same text, different objects); there is no Object Type filter in the Audit Trail yet, so none was changed.

## LEAVE-GUARD-2026-11-17 — screens that keep edits until Save ask before they leave
**Owner request (2026-11-17):** the project already has a modal that asks before the user leaves a page without saving ("Leave without saving?", `NavigationGuardModal`); apply it to the screens that need it so nothing is lost by leaving.
**Built:** one hook, `useLeaveGuard(dirty, title, destination)` (moved from Notifications to `src/hooks`), gives `go(path | step, options)` for Back, Cancel, a breadcrumb and a link, `confirmLeave(action)` for a way out that is not a path, and the modal; it also reports the changes to the shell (`useReportUnsavedChanges`), so the sidebar asks and a closed tab or a reload asks. `useFormDirty(values, ready)` is the starting point for forms that have no saved copy to compare with. The question reads the same in the sidebar and in the screens ("Leave without saving?", "Stay on page", "Leave Anyway"); the four Notifications screens that said "Discard unsaved changes?" now say it too. The move that follows a successful Save is not a leave and does not ask.
**Applied to (30 screens):** E-Sign Config, Document Properties, Controlled Copies Policy, Uncontrolled Copies Policy, Training Properties, Document Name Format, Document Component, Knowledge Category Hierarchy, Knowledge Category Component, Report Appearance, Report Name Format, Report Properties, Report Template, Scheduled Report, Access Profile Details, Shared Permission Set, Object Access Rule, SoD Constraint, Transition Policy and State Policy (Workflow Authorization), Duplicate Policy, Time-Limited User (new and edit), Personal Information, Preferences (also a change of tab), Education School, System Announcement, Propose Policy Change (Backup), Training Requirement / Assessment / Job Assignment / Matrix Version, New Training Activity, New Job Assignment; already guarded: New Document, Request Controlled Copy, SoD list, Add User, the Notifications editors, Configuration pages.
**Not covered, and why:** the Publishing Template editor, the Document Revision workspace and the Document detail (large screens with no unsaved-change state of their own: each needs its own definition of "changed"); screens that edit in a modal on a list (Object Numbering, dictionaries) are left to the modal's own Cancel; the breadcrumb inside the Backup administration pages is a shared component that is not guarded; the browser's Back button is not intercepted (the app uses `BrowserRouter`).
**Status:** built on DEV, not committed. Record: `validation/CONFIGURATION_PAGES_SPLIT_2026_11_17_CHANGE_RECORD.md` (addendum).

## SECURITY-AUTHORIZATION-REVIEW-2026-11-18 — what Security & Authorization still needs after the new modules (owner decisions, each chosen from options the owner ticked)

**Why.** The system gained Training, Reports, Backup and many Configuration pages since the first catalogue of Security & Authorization. A review of source (not of the DEV database) found: SoD rules only for Document Control and for user/role administration; an Access Review that shows status, profiles, permission count and the super-admin flag only; no reset of a person's MFA by an administrator; no use of the last sign-in date; no way to ask "who holds permission X"; no delegation of a task while its holder is away.

**Basis offered to the owner:** EU-GMP Annex 11 §12 (access limited and recorded, §12.3 changes of access recorded), 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(d)(g) and §11.300(c) (loss management of authentication devices: rigorous controls before a replacement), PIC/S and MHRA data-integrity guidance (periodic review of access, segregation of duties, inactive accounts). Market QMS practice was mentioned in general terms and not verified product by product.

**Decisions (owner, 2026-11-18).**
- **D1 SoD seeding: by risk.** A pair that lets a person approve or verify their own work is BLOCK; a weaker pair is WARN. Both stay switchable (e-signed, audited) in Segregation of Duties.
- **D2 Pairs wanted:** Training (assign vs complete/approve), Reports (author vs GMP approval), Backup (request vs approve/execute), Access Profile (assign vs Access Review decision).
  - **Amendment found while building (to confirm with the owner):** the Backup restore request/approve/execute pairs are **not** seeded, because the owner already decided on 2026-11-09 (BACKUP-RESTORE-SIMPLIFIED-2026-11-09) that restore approval is on paper and the software has no such flow; a rule over it would govern a step that does not exist. The two Backup pairs that exist in the software are seeded instead: policy propose vs approve (a second person is already required) and backup export vs export approve.
- **D3 Access Review gets four more facts per person:** last sign-in and days inactive; MFA state; time-limited grants with their end date; open SoD violations.
- **D4 Reset MFA by an administrator, with a typed reason and an electronic signature.** Sessions of the person are revoked, the person is told by e-mail and enrols again at the next sign-in, everything is audited.
- **D5 Inactive accounts: flag and report only.** No automatic suspension. The threshold is a setting; the flag shows in Access Review and User Management.
- **D6 Access Explorer:** a screen of its own in User Administration to ask "who holds this permission" (profiles, permission sets, people, with the source of the grant and any end date), filterable and exportable.
- **D7 Delegation: time-limited, for one kind of task, ticked by the owner.** The substitute can do only that task, is still checked for SoD and base permission, signs under their own name, and the audit says "acting for". Because it touches the participant model of live modules, **the Participant and SoD Catalog row must be approved before any code**; design recorded in `docs/AUTHORIZATION_PARTICIPANT_SOD_CATALOG.md`.
- **D8 Access requests (ask, approve, grant): not now.**

**Status (2026-11-18).** Built, not applied to any database, not committed: **D1, D2** (migration V802; `SodSeedV802MigrationTest`), **D3 and D5 in Access Review** (migration V803; `AccessReviewService.captureFacts`, `SodConstraintService.countActiveViolations`, `SystemConfigurationService.inactiveAccountDays`, the threshold field in Configuration > Security > Sessions & Accounts; tests `AccessReviewFactsTest`, `InactiveAccountThresholdTest`, `AccessReviewCampaignDetailView.facts.test.tsx`). Record: `validation/SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_REVIEW_2026_11_18_CHANGE_RECORD.md`.
**Built later the same day (not applied to any database, not committed):**
- **D4 Reset MFA:** `UserManagementService.resetMfa`, `POST /settings/users/{id}/reset-mfa`, migration V804 (signature meaning `USER_MFA_RESET`), mandatory e-mail-only event `security.account_mfa_reset`, capability `resetMfa`, screen modal with a reason and the electronic signature. Decided while building: **no new permission** - it uses `settings.user.reset_password` (both recover another person's credentials); a separate permission can be added later. The person's own account is refused; nothing changes before the signature is valid; the authenticator and the trusted devices are switched off, every session is signed out, the person is told, and the audit record carries the signature.
- **D5 in User Management:** the Inactive flag (list status, profile summary, Account Status tab) from the same threshold; Active accounts only.
- **D6 Access Explorer:** `AccessExplorerService`, `GET /security/access-explorer/permissions|holders`, screen under User Administration, open to whoever may view users or access profiles (it shows nothing the user list does not). **Export to a file is not built**: an export of who-holds-what must itself be audited, which needs a new audit object type in the registry; to be decided.
**Not built:** **D7 Delegation.** The catalog row is written as a proposal in `docs/AUTHORIZATION_PARTICIPANT_SOD_CATALOG.md` with its open questions; the rule of that file says nothing is built before BA/QA/Compliance approve it, and the change touches the participant model of live modules and the meaning of a signature. D8 not now, as decided.
**Effect to know before applying V802:** a BLOCK pair stops a *new* assignment of a profile to a person who, with it, would hold both permissions; a person who already holds both through today's profiles is not changed, but a further profile cannot be assigned to them until the conflict is removed (the rule of `AccessProfileService.requireNoBlockingSodConflict`, unchanged). Run the SoD scan after applying and read its list before anyone is blocked unexpectedly. Each of the ten rows can be switched off in Segregation of Duties (e-signed, audited).

**SECURITY-AUTHORIZATION-REVIEW real test on DEV (2026-11-18, same entry).** DEV rebuilt (backend and frontend) with the work above; logged in as `admin`; a throwaway user `uat.reset.test` made through the application, a test Access Review campaign ARC-2026-0003; both removed afterwards (user Terminated, campaign Cancelled; their audit records stay).
- **Verified for real:** V802 to V804 applied on Postgres (this also happened earlier, by the Spring test run, which shares the DEV database); Reset Password opens on a suggestion that follows the real rules, an edited short password turns Confirm off with the failed rules listed in red, the e-mail carries the temporary password and that password signs in; Reset MFA signs with the reason, sets `mfa_enabled` false, writes `USER_MFA_RESET` and is no longer offered afterwards; Access Review shows last sign-in, MFA, time-limited, SoD conflicts and Inactive; Access Explorer lists the holders of `training.paper.verify` with the profile and set; the Configuration tabs and their History tab show the real audit records.
- **Found only by the real run, and fixed:** (1) the Reset MFA address was missing from the controller although every service test passed (test added: `SettingsUserControllerResetMfaTest`); (2) Access Explorer search needed the exact phrase ("paper verify" found nothing): every word must now match, in any order; (3) a PowerShell edit had turned the quotes of the slide editor into mojibake (file repaired; PowerShell `Set-Content` must not be used on these source files); (4) the signature window asks for the reason itself, so the Reset MFA window no longer asks for it twice; (5) the Inactive badge was clipped in the narrow status column.
- **Effect to know:** the ten SoD rules are live on DEV. The BLOCK pairs hit `admin`, `approver.test1` and `user.a.test` (all Training permissions through the DEV test permission set), and `admin` and `sys.admin2` for Backup policy; nothing was changed for them, but no further access profile can be assigned to them until the conflict is removed.

**SECURITY-AUTHORIZATION-REVIEW, the three open items decided (owner, 2026-11-18, ticked from options).**
- **D7 Delegation: not now.** Substitution stays what it is today (Time-Limited User and changing the participant of a record by hand). The proposed catalog row stays unapproved in `docs/AUTHORIZATION_PARTICIPANT_SOD_CATALOG.md`.
- **Export of Access Explorer: through Reports.** New Report Type **Permission Holders** (`PermissionHolderReportMetadataProvider`, category Security): one row for each permission, access profile and person, built from the same chain the system decides with (ACTIVE profile, ACTIVE permission set). Used by whoever may view users or access profiles, template given to the whole system scope only, so the run is authorized, audited and kept like every other report. Access Explorer says so on its screen. No export button of its own, so no new audit object type was needed.
- **Reset MFA has its own permission** `settings.user.reset_mfa` (V805), given to every permission set that holds Reset Password (7 sets on DEV, the same 8 people), so nobody lost the ability. It decides the capability and the endpoint; holding Reset Password alone is no longer enough.
- **Found by the real run:** V805 first failed on DEV (`relation "permission_dependencies" does not exist`: V747 dropped that table, and the migration copied an older pattern that wrote to it). The failed migration rolled back and left the backend restarting until the insert was removed (the migration had not been applied, so it could be corrected); a text test now keeps that table out of it. Lesson: a migration copied from an older one must be checked against the migrations since.
- **Verified for real on DEV (2026-11-18):** V805 applied; `reset_mfa` held by the same 7 sets and 8 people as `reset_password`; Reset MFA through the new permission on a throwaway user (capability names `settings.user.reset_mfa`, 204, MFA off, audit `USER_MFA_RESET`), user terminated afterwards; the Report Type is listed in the Reports platform; its SQL runs on Postgres (3 rows, 3 distinct identities for `training.paper.verify`, the same 3 people Access Explorer shows). A full report run from a template was not done.

## LEAVE-GUARD-2026-11-18 — addendum: dialogs, browser Back, Publishing Template editor

- **Dialogs:** `FormModal` has an optional `dirty` prop. When true, closing by X, backdrop, Esc or Cancel asks "Discard unsaved changes?" (Keep editing / Discard Changes); Save is not a close. `useOpenedFormDirty` gives `dirty` for dialogs that fill their form from an item in an effect. Applied to: Object Numbering, Departments, Business Units, Positions, Storage Locations, Retention Policies, Document Types, Document Sub-Types, Degree Levels, Schools (dialog, unused by the list), Work Positions.
- **Publishing Template editor:** asks on Back and breadcrumb when name, description, status or mode differ from what was loaded/saved. Uploads, deletes and placeholder styles save by their own actions and are not "unsaved".
- **Browser Back (owner decision 2026-11-18, option "data router"):** `index.tsx` now uses `createBrowserRouter` with one splat route that holds the unchanged `<Routes>` tree. `UnsavedChangesPopGuard` (`useBlocker`) asks on a browser Back/Forward (POP only) while a screen reports unsaved changes (`useReportUnsavedChanges`). A leave a screen has just confirmed itself (`allowNextLeave`) is let through without a second question. Pushes (links, sidebar) stay with the screens' own guards.
- **Document Revision workspace / Document detail (owner decision 2026-11-18: attach only where there is a direct-edit form):** neither screen has a form with its own Save (they act by workflow actions with e-signature), so nothing was attached. New Document and Request Controlled Copy already had their own guards.
- **Audit Object Type names "Notifications" / "Security" (owner decision 2026-11-18):** kept as they are.
- **Not decided yet:** per-page permissions for the 8 Configuration pages (owner chose "separate permission for all 8 pages"); needs the design questions recorded in the change record before any code.
- **Evidence:** tsc clean; vitest 324 files / 1872 tests pass; Edge on DEV (admin, dco.test1, approver.test1): 9 dialogs (new and edit-existing rows) 5 steps each, Report Appearance, Publishing Template, Announcement, New Backup Policy, and 10 checks for browser Back/Forward/Stay/Leave/Cancel-once/deep-link reload. Not run: Spring integration tests; GitNexus `impact`/`detect_changes` (not available in the session).

## CONFIGURATION-PERMISSIONS-PER-PAGE-2026-11-18 — one View and one Manage permission for each page of Configuration

- **Decisions (owner, 2026-11-18, all approved in the session):** (1) two permissions per page, View and Manage; (2) everybody who held `settings.configuration.view` / `.manage` receives all the new permissions automatically, so nobody loses anything; (3) the old pair is **retired completely in the same change** (the owner kept this choice after being shown that the old pair was also a fallback in about 80 places of about 35 backend files, and chose it over a two-step plan); (4) a save that changes several pages needs the Manage permission of every page it changes, otherwise the whole save is refused; (5) the owner approved the whole change.
- **Consequences recorded:** two screens that are not pages of Configuration had only the old pair, so they got permissions of their own: `settings.electronic_signature.view/manage` (E-Sign Config) and `settings.system_info.view` (System Information). The screens that accepted the old pair as a fallback (Reports, Document Administration, Notifications Administration, Object Numbering, Controlled / Uncontrolled Copies policy, Publishing Templates, Document Properties) now accept only their own permissions; the people who held the old pair are given those own permissions by V806 so that they keep their access.
- **Codes:** `settings.config_<page>.view|manage`, `<page>` = general, navigation_labels, security, notifications, integrations, preview_file, image_watermark, features (single source in the backend: `ConfigurationAuditPage`). The History tab of a page in the Audit Trail follows the View of that page.
- **Trap found and handled:** the trigger `validate_emergency_access_guard` (V693) requires the emergency account of an enabled network policy to hold the old Manage; V806 replaces it with `settings.config_security.manage` before deleting the old permission.
- **Record:** `eqms-backend/docs/validation/CONFIGURATION_PERMISSIONS_PER_PAGE_2026_11_18_CHANGE_RECORD.md`.

## AUTH-HYBRID-PHASE-A-B-2026-11-18 — phases A and B of the system-wide Hybrid authorization plan

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-18):** approve phase A (foundation and governance, no behavior change) and then phase B (Report as the pilot) of `docs/AUTHORIZATION_SYSTEM_WIDE_HYBRID_PLAN_2026_11_15.md`. Order stays A, B, then C (Training), then D-F. Permission codes stay as they are during the move (aliases).
- **Why:** the screens Workflow Authorization, Workflow Role Catalog, Engine Diagnostics and Object Access Rules cover only Document Control (45 action policies and 16 state policies, all `DOCUMENT_CONTROL`; object access rules and their actions are document concepts), while Training (237 routes), Report (65), Backup, Access Review and Audit Review decide by code.
- **Reclassification:** Training and Report are **Live (catalog pending approval)** in `AUTHORIZATION_MODULE_INVENTORY.md`. The rule of that file still holds: no policy, participant or permission is added to them until their Action Catalog rows are approved.
- **Done in this step (no behavior change):** `docs/AUTHORIZATION_ACTION_CATALOG_REPORT_AS_IS_2026_11_18.md` (As-Is, every row traced to a method). **Open:** each row needs "approve as is" or "change" from the owner/QA; the "Cần quyết" column lists what the code cannot answer (for example whether a GMP template needs a second approver, and what happens to a schedule whose owner lost the permission).
- **Next, after the rows are approved:** (1) engine: policy variant dimension (GMP / non-GMP), runtime "different person" relation check in place of `ReportTemplateService.requireIndependentReviewer`, adapter based on the shared policy table; (2) architecture test "every route touches a checker or is on the exception list"; (3) Report adapter, seeded policies from the catalog, shadow run, flag, removal of the old code. Each with GitNexus `impact` before editing and `detect_changes` before commit.
- **Also found while checking the four screens:** Workflow Role Catalog takes the Module of a role as free text (placeholder `document-control`, seeded values `documents` and `training`, workflows use `DOCUMENT_CONTROL`): three spellings for one idea and no validation. To be fixed together with the module vocabulary in phase A. The list on Object Access Rules is empty on purpose: it shows only central rules, the three rules on DEV belong to departments / business units and are managed with Department visibility.

- **Action Catalog Report duyệt (owner, 2026-11-18):** mọi dòng duyệt **như hiện trạng** (To-Be = As-Is): duyệt GMP một người khác tác giả và người gửi; gỡ legal hold một người có chữ ký; chủ lịch mất quyền thì dừng lịch và báo quản lý lịch (mã đã làm đúng, `processOneDueSchedule`). Không có dòng nào đổi hành vi, nên khi Report chuyển sang động cơ, shadow phải cho 0 lệch.
- **Chặn cần bạn đồng ý rõ (CRITICAL):** bước A.2 sửa `AuthorizationEngineService.authorize` (GitNexus `impact`: CRITICAL, 69 symbol, 14 luồng, 6 nơi gọi trực tiếp; `ResourceAuthorizationAdapter` MEDIUM, 10). Thiết kế cộng thêm, mặc định không đổi hành vi: (1) `ResolvedPolicy` thêm `forbiddenRelationCodes` (kiểm tra khác người lúc chạy: người duyệt không được giữ quan hệ AUTHOR hay SUBMITTER); (2) adapter thêm `resolveVariant(resourceId)` và `resolvePolicy(action, state, documentTypeId, variant)` (mặc định gọi hàm cũ; Report dùng variant GMP / không GMP). Không có chỗ nào đổi kết quả của Document, Revision, Controlled Copy, User. Chưa sửa dòng mã nào.

- **Done 2026-11-18 (owner agreed to the CRITICAL change):** (A.2) `ResolvedPolicy` has `forbiddenRelationCodes` (runtime "a different person"; engine reason `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`, checked after permission, scope and required relations) and the adapter interface has `resolveVariant` / `resolvePolicy(..., variant)`; both are additive with defaults, so Document, Revision, Controlled Copy and User decide exactly as before (22 engine tests, 7 cutover and adapter test classes pass). (B.1) `ReportTemplateResourceAdapter` (resource type `REPORT_TEMPLATE`, actions `APPROVE` and `REJECT`, variant `GMP`) decides what `ReportTemplateService.approve / reject` decide in code; the engine is asked in the **shadow** from `reviewChecks`, a disagreement of allow / deny is recorded in `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events`, and the outcome never changes. Matrix test: 768 cells (path of review x state x GMP live or by the revision x permission x relation x action) agree with the approved rules.
- **Not done yet in phase B (next):** the other Report actions of the catalog (submit, upgrade, enable, run, schedule, legal hold, ...); policies in `workflow_action_policies` and the Report workflow in the registry so Workflow Authorization / Engine Diagnostics / Engine Health list the module and can edit it; an observation window with 0 mismatches; the cutover flag; removal of `requireIndependentReviewer` and the local `require` in the Report services.

- **Done 2026-11-18, second step of phase B (owner: "finish it before stopping"):** (V807) `workflow_action_policy_relations.forbidden` plus the relations `AUTHOR` and `SUBMITTER` of `REPORT_TEMPLATE`, and four system policies (module `REPORT`, workflow `REPORT_TEMPLATE`: APPROVE, APPROVE_GMP, REJECT, REJECT_GMP, from `PENDING_APPROVAL`, each forbidding AUTHOR and SUBMITTER); the adapter reads them from `workflow_action_policies`, so Workflow Authorization (Transition Policies shows "not AUTHOR", Action Matrix, Policy Resolver), Engine Diagnostics (Simulator by template code, Relation Definitions, Engine Health) list Report beside Document Control and an administrator can edit it (e-signed, audited; new `PUT /authorization/workflow-policies/{id}/forbidden-relations`). The required-relations editor no longer turns a forbidden relation into a required one. (Cutover) `ReportTemplateService.reviewChecks`: flag `REPORT_TEMPLATE` in `APP_AUTHORIZATION_HYBRID_ENGINE_ENABLED_RESOURCE_TYPES` makes the engine decide (old checks as comparator, errors keep the codes the screens know); flag off (the default, and DEV now) the old checks decide and the engine runs in the shadow. Bug found by the new tests: the shadow ignored the refusal "the approver is the author" because it compared `getMessage()` instead of `getReason()`; fixed. (Workflow Role Catalog) the Module is now a list (documents, training, reports, backup, security, audit-trail) and the server refuses any other value (it was free text with three spellings). (Object Access Rules) the screen says what it covers (documents and Knowledge Base) and where other modules are decided.
- **Not enabled yet:** the cutover flag stays OFF on DEV. Evidence still missing: a real approval / return by a person who holds `reports.definition.approve` (only `approver.test1` does on DEV, and its password was not given for this work), and an observation window with 0 mismatches in the shadow. The rest of the Report catalog (run, schedule, legal hold, ...) and Training, Backup, Access Review remain phases B-C.
- **Real run on DEV (2026-11-18, owner gave `approver.test1`):** admin submitted RPT.0006; admin's return was refused (no approval permission); `approver.test1` returned it to Draft with an e-signature (200); `approver.test1` submitted RPT.0005 and its own return was refused with `REPORT_APPROVER_IS_AUTHOR` (409). Shadow rows for `REPORT_TEMPLATE`: 0 before and after, so the engine and the old checks agreed on every one of these decisions. RPT.0005 was left in Pending Approval (nobody else holds the approval permission on DEV to return it). The cutover flag is still OFF.

- **Action Catalog As-Is for Training written (2026-11-18):** `docs/AUTHORIZATION_ACTION_CATALOG_TRAINING_AS_IS_2026_11_18.md` (mechanism, the 12 approval steps, 7 lifecycles, SoD, mapping to the engine). It is As-Is, **not approved**: no policy, participant or permission is added to Training until the owner / QA approves the rows. Found while writing it: three approval steps do not run (`RECORD_VOID` and `JOB_ASSIGN_SIGN` are in `training_approval_modes` but not in `STEPS`; `SPECIMEN_CONFIRM` is in `STEPS` but the Specimen service never uses it), and two SoD rules are WARN only (content review vs approve, assign vs close). Six decisions are listed in section 6 of the catalog.

- **Catalog TRAINING approved as it is (owner, 2026-11-18)**, including the three steps that do not run (left as they are) and the WARN level of two SoD rules. **First part of the Training adapters built:** the Requirement (`TRAINING_REQUIREMENT`: SUBMIT, REVIEW, APPROVE, REJECT) and the Matrix version (`TRAINING_MATRIX_VERSION`: SUBMIT, APPROVE, REJECT). V810: relations (PREPARER, REVIEWED_BY, REVIEW_ASSIGNEE, APPROVAL_ASSIGNEE) and 8 system policies with required and forbidden relations; `TrainingPolicyAdapter` (permission of the step, department scope, the right to designate the next person, the person designated, the people kept apart), `TrainingShadow` around the service methods (the service still decides; a disagreement of allow / deny is written to the shadow table; failures that are not about who may act are not compared). Workflow Authorization, Policy Resolver, Simulator, Relation Definitions and Engine Health list Training. Matrix test: 8192 cells for the requirement and 384 for the matrix version agree with the approved rules; the migration is checked against the registry and the relation table.
- **Not done in Training:** Activity (about 25 actions, the largest), Assessment, Job assignment, Personnel review, Specimen, record export, vendor approval and the settings; the cutover flag for Training resource types (shadow only now); a real approval by two people on DEV (needs training users with review / approve permissions and a draft requirement). `TrainingAccess.require` (CRITICAL) was not changed.

- **Training Activity adapter built (2026-11-18, owner: continue with Activity):** V811 (generated with the registry of defaults from one table, so they cannot differ) adds 26 system policies for 14 actions on the activity itself (UPDATE, ADD_SESSION, ADD_PARTICIPANTS, CONFIRM_LIST, NOTIFY, CANCEL, REOPEN_CONTENT, SUPPLEMENTARY, CONTENT_REVIEW, CONTENT_APPROVE, SEND_ATTENDANCE_LIST, FINISH, CONFIRM_COMPLETION, RETURN_FOR_CORRECTION) and 6 relations (SENDER, CONTENT_CREATOR, CONTENT_REVIEWER, CONTENT_REVIEW_ASSIGNEE, CONTENT_APPROVE_ASSIGNEE, LIST_PREPARER). `TrainingActivityResourceAdapter` mirrors `isSender` (a trainer acts with no permission and no department; a session without a trainer falls back to conduct / organize in the department), the department rule, the people kept apart and the designated people; the 13 service methods are wrapped by `TrainingShadow` (the service still decides). Matrix test: 172,032 cells (6 states x 16 permission sets x 64 relation sets x scope x 14 actions) agree with the approved rules; migration, registry and relation rows are checked against each other. Actions on one participant (attendance, reading, check-in, absence) are a different resource and are not done. The cutover flag for Training resource types still does not exist (shadow only).

- **Every other module brought onto the Hybrid engine, as it is in code (owner directive 2026-11-18: "do every module, finish before stopping, test as you go"):** four migrations and one adapter per lifecycle, all generated from the same table as the registry of defaults so the two cannot differ.
  - V812 Training: 10 workflows (Participant, Assessment, Job assignment, Personnel review, Record export, Record export batch, Trainer exception, Suspension, Vendor, Record), 40 system policies, 45 relation rows. V813 Report: Schedule (pause, resume, delete, change owner from any state), Run (retry from Failed / Retry Scheduled), File (place / release legal hold). V814 Security Review: Access Review and Audit Trail Review campaigns (decide item, complete, cancel from In Progress). V815 Uncontrolled Copy (distribute, cancel from Ready for Distribution).
  - The rules that mix a permission with ownership or scope are relations computed by the adapter exactly as the service computes them (MAY_MANAGE, MAY_RETRY, HOLD_RIGHT, CONDUCTOR, DESIGNER_OR_APPROVER, CLOSER_OK, REASSIGN_RIGHT). Nothing changes in who may do what.
  - **Shadow only.** The services still decide; the engine is asked first through `ShadowGuard` / `TrainingShadow` and a disagreement is written to `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events`. No cutover flag exists for these resource types; a cutover is a separate decision with its own evidence.
  - **Backup & Restore is an exception, not a gap:** its lifecycle lives in the external backup agent (`BackupAgentClient`), not in this repository, so there is no state here for an adapter to read. It stays flat permissions plus the Segregation of Duties rule "Propose vs Approve Backup Policy Change".
  - **Left as flat permissions on purpose (plan 7.A):** dictionaries, notification administration, settings, Training settings / annex / code rules, Knowledge (object rules), Executed Record (uses the Controlled Copy engine).
  - **Evidence:** matrix tests check every cell of state x permissions x relations x scope x action against a hand-written rule (`TrainingOtherLifecyclesMatrixTest` 9 tests, `ReportAndReviewMatrixTest` 5 tests, plus the Training Requirement, Matrix and Activity tests of the earlier steps) and that the registry equals the migration. Backend suite: 3016 tests, 6 failures, all six identical on a clean checkout of HEAD (data on the shared DEV database: `ControlledCopyRecallByScanIntegrationTest` x2, `SelfServiceHomePageAndPermissionTest`, `UpgradeRevisionIntegrationTest`, `DocumentObsoleteUpgradeProductionPathConcurrencyTest` x2); `DocumentListVisibilityParityIntegrationTest` hangs on the DEV database and was excluded. DEV: Flyway 806-815 succeeded; the Simulator (`/authorization/evaluate`, admin) allowed the action in the right state and refused it in the wrong one for Training job assignment, Personnel review, Training record, Report schedule, run and file; no shadow row for any new resource type.
  - **Not verified:** a real two-person flow for each lifecycle (needs Training review / approval accounts), the Access / Audit campaigns and the Uncontrolled Copy in an In Progress / Ready state (none exist on DEV), and the cutover itself.

- **Cutover of the new resource types, DEV only (owner, 2026-11-18: "do the cutover for each type"):** flag `APP_AUTHORIZATION_HYBRID_ENGINE_ENABLED_RESOURCE_TYPES` in `docker-compose.yml` now lists REPORT_TEMPLATE, REPORT_SCHEDULE, REPORT_RUN, REPORT_ARTIFACT, ACCESS_REVIEW_CAMPAIGN, AUDIT_REVIEW_CAMPAIGN, UNCONTROLLED_COPY and the 14 Training types. **What cutover means here:** `ShadowGuard` / `TrainingShadow` stop a step, before anything changes, when the engine refuses the person for a reason about the actor (`MISSING_PERMISSION`, `OUT_OF_SCOPE`, `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`, `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`, `USER_NOT_ACTIVE`) with an `AccessDeniedException` carrying the engine's code. When the engine allows, the check the service has always made still runs after it, so **the engine can only make a step stricter than the code, never looser**; a policy edited in Workflow Authorization to be looser does not take effect until the old check is removed. A refusal about the state of the record is left to the service so the screens keep their error codes. The old checks were **not deleted** (unlike REVISION and USER lifecycle actions): rollback is to remove the type from the flag and restart.
  - **Evidence:** `ShadowGuardCutoverTest` (flag on / off / another type, each refusal reason, state refusal, allow then the service refuses, Training wrapper); 1072 related tests pass. DEV: `approver.test1` was stopped by the engine on Training record void (403 `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`) where `admin` reached the service's own validation (400); no shadow row.
  - **Side effect of the verification, restored:** the DEV test paused schedules SRP-2026-00001 and -00002 and placed a legal hold on one report file as `approver.test1` (allowed by its permissions). Both schedules were resumed (their state before is assumed Active; the audit shows the pause came from the test) and the hold was released by `admin` (the placer may not release). The pauses, the hold and the release are in the audit trail.
  - **Not done:** PROD flag (this PC is DEV only), observation window on real traffic before any production use, and removing the old checks so a looser policy can take effect.

- **Old checks deleted, type by type (owner, 2026-11-18: "delete the old checks, one type at a time"):** for 16 resource types the permission / scope / person checks were removed from the services and the Hybrid engine is the **only** decision about who may act: UNCONTROLLED_COPY, ACCESS_REVIEW_CAMPAIGN, AUDIT_REVIEW_CAMPAIGN, REPORT_SCHEDULE, REPORT_RUN, REPORT_ARTIFACT, TRAINING_ASSESSMENT, TRAINING_JOB_ASSIGNMENT, TRAINING_PERSONNEL_REVIEW, TRAINING_RECORD, TRAINING_SUSPENSION, TRAINING_VENDOR, TRAINING_PARTICIPANT, TRAINING_ACTIVITY, TRAINING_REQUIREMENT and TRAINING_MATRIX_VERSION. They are listed in `ShadowGuard.ENGINE_DECIDES` (a constant, like REVISION and the USER lifecycle actions before them), **not governed by the flag any more**, and fail closed: no engine, an error of the engine, or any refusal stops the step (`AccessDeniedException` carrying the engine's code). The only exception is a record that does not exist (no state): the service answers not found. **Rollback is no longer a flag:** it means restoring the deleted checks from history.
  - **What stays in the services:** the state of the record, the version, the reason, the signature, and every step that signs an approval assignment (`approvals.requireCanSign` of Requirement review/approve, Matrix approve, Activity content / completion, Job assignment exception, Record export, Export batch, Trainer exception, and the closer of a Personnel review): that call also returns the assignment the signature completes, so it cannot be a pure check. For those steps the engine decides first and the assignment check still runs after it. **Not cut over:** REPORT_TEMPLATE (own flag path, the old checks run as the comparator), TRAINING_RECORD_EXPORT, TRAINING_RECORD_EXPORT_BATCH, TRAINING_TRAINER_EXCEPTION (only signature steps: nothing to delete), Backup (external agent).
  - **Behavior changes the owner should know:** (1) a step on a record in the wrong state is refused by the engine with 403 `POLICY_NOT_CONFIGURED` where the service used to answer 409 with its own code, for these types; (2) the messages "You do not have permission to ..." are now the engine's code (`MISSING_PERMISSION`, `OUT_OF_SCOPE`, `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`, `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`); (3) a policy edited in Workflow Authorization to be looser now takes effect for these types, and a policy made inactive blocks the action.
  - **A guarantee kept on purpose:** the retry of a report run used the permissions as they are now (not the cache) so that a withdrawn grant cannot authorize; the relation `MAY_RETRY` of REPORT_RUN reads them the same way (`ReportRunRetryStaleGrantTest`). The rule of the schedule ownership (`ReportScheduleOwnershipScopeTest`) is tested on the adapter.
  - **Evidence:** 1042 related tests pass; the unit tests of the services that asserted a deleted check were rewritten to give the service an engine that allows (`TestGuards`) or refuses (the engine's refusal stops the step before anything is signed); `ShadowGuardCutoverTest` covers fail-closed. DEV after rebuild: Training record void (admin reaches the service's validation, `approver.test1` is refused by the engine with `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`), personnel review close (both reach the service), Uncontrolled Copy distribute and cancel on a Distributed copy (403 `POLICY_NOT_CONFIGURED` for both, no change), a missing record (not found), no audit row written by the refusals.
  - **Not verified:** a real two-person flow per type after the deletion, the screens' handling of 403 where they expected 409 (buttons come from capabilities, but a stale page may show the engine's code), and an observation window on real traffic: the shadow table only records disagreements, and there is little traffic on DEV for these types. This is DEV only; the owner decides when, and whether, PROD follows.

- **The last four types cut over (owner, 2026-11-18: "do the remaining four"):** REPORT_TEMPLATE, TRAINING_RECORD_EXPORT, TRAINING_RECORD_EXPORT_BATCH and TRAINING_TRAINER_EXCEPTION are now decided by the engine alone (the last three are in `ShadowGuard.ENGINE_DECIDES`: their only check was the signature step `approvals.requireCanSign`, which stays because it also returns the assignment the signature completes, so the engine decides first and that check runs after it). **Every resource type of Training and Report now has the engine as the first and fail-closed decision.** Backup stays outside (external agent).
  - **REPORT_TEMPLATE:** `ReportTemplateService` no longer has the flag, the comparator, the shadow record or the old gates (`legacyReviewChecks`, `requireReviewPermission`, `requireIndependentReviewer`); `reviewChecks` and `requireByPolicy` ask the engine and fail closed (no engine or an error refuses). The refusals keep the codes the screens know (`REPORT_APPROVER_IS_AUTHOR`, `REPORT_GMP_APPROVER_REQUIRED`, `REPORT_SCOPE_DENIED`, `REPORT_TEMPLATE_NOT_PENDING`). One case keeps a permission check on purpose: deleting a GMP template (the engine has no policy for it; the service refuses it with its own error after asking for the permission, as before). The flag value `REPORT_TEMPLATE` in `docker-compose.yml` is now unused. **Accepted difference:** the permission of a template action is read from the permission cache (cleared whenever an Access Profile changes) and not from the effective permissions at the moment of the call as the old gate did.
  - **Evidence:** 994 related tests pass (the rules of the approval, the GMP variant and the person kept apart are the matrix of `ReportTemplateResourceAdapterTest`; `ReportTemplateApprovalTest` and `ReportTemplateRevisionActionsTest` now give the service an engine that allows or refuses). DEV after rebuild: approving RPT.0005 and a draft template with an empty reason, as `admin` (no approval permission: 403 `REPORT_SCOPE_DENIED`) and as `approver.test1` (409 `REPORT_APPROVER_IS_AUTHOR`, and 409 `REPORT_TEMPLATE_NOT_PENDING`); no data or audit row changed.
  - **Not verified:** a real approval and a real return of a template after the deletion, and the three Training steps with two real people.

- **Follow-up of the Security & Authorization audit (owner, 2026-11-18: "do all of it"):**
  1. **Buttons from the engine.** `EngineCapabilities` (new) makes the capability maps of the details of Training Requirement, Matrix version, Activity, Assessment, Job assignment, Personnel review, of Uncontrolled Copy, and of the Report template follow the same engine decision that is made when the person presses the button. For a key whose old hint was only permission, scope and state the key is replaced by the engine's answer (so a policy edited to be looser shows the button); for a key that also holds a business condition both must agree. An engine that fails turns the key off. **Limits:** lists still use the old hints (an engine call for each row was judged too heavy), and the screens of the Access / Audit Review campaigns and of Report schedule, run and file still hide or show by permission (equal to the default policies; they can differ from a policy edited away from the default).
  2. **Warning before changing a policy.** For the types whose old checks were deleted, the preview of an update returns `ENGINE_DECIDES_ALONE` (a change takes effect at once for everyone), `DEACTIVATING_STOPS_THE_ACTION` (policy inactive) and `NO_PERMISSION_AND_NO_ACTOR`; the policy carries `ENGINE_DECIDES_ALONE` in its warnings and the Deactivate confirmation says that nobody could then do the action. The row badge "Warning" ignores that code.
  3. **Documents.** `SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_USER_GUIDE.md` has section 10 (what the engine decides, the refusal codes, what to check before editing a policy, how the buttons work, that rollback is by code); `AUTHORIZATION_VERIFICATION_STATUS.md` was brought to 2026-11-18 (older rows marked as not re-checked); `AUTHORIZATION_GLOBAL_ACTION_CATALOG.md` points to the Training and Report catalogs.
  4. **More actions through the engine (V816).** Seven more actions of the Training Activity: hold a session, record attendance, postpone a session, change a session, set the content exemptions, record an assessment result, designate the approvers of a step (12 policies, relation `CONDUCTOR` computed by the adapter, `DESIGNATE` applies in every state as the service does). Their old checks were deleted. Matrix test `TrainingActivityActionsMatrixTest`; DEV: the Simulator answers as the rules say for activities in Planned, Completed and Reserved. **What is not wrapped, and why:** creating a record (no record exists for the engine to read), reading and listing, the images of specimens, and the Specimen has no lifecycle step yet (the confirm step is only declared). Those keep their checks in the services.
  - **The three small items of that list, done afterwards (2026-11-18, "continue"):** (1) the Simulator finds a record by number or name (`GET /authorization/resource-lookup`, fixed statements per type in `AuthorizationResourceLookupService`, the search text is the only parameter; same gate as the evaluation; 23 types verified on DEV, Report template stays by code) instead of a pasted UUID; (2) Engine Health shows, per resource type, how many **real** steps the engine decided since the backend started, allowed and refused (`EngineEvaluationCounters`, counted in `ShadowGuard`, `TrainingShadow` and the Report template gate; **in memory: it starts again at zero when the backend restarts**, and the screen says since when; simulations and buttons are not counted); (3) the Permission Holders button of Access Explorer (list and detail) opens the run page of the template made from that Report Type with the permission filled in (`?filter.permission=`), or says that no such template is available to the person and opens the Report Library; the export still goes through Reports, so it is authorized, audited and kept like any report. **Found by the full run and fixed:** `ShadowGuard.engineDecides(null)` threw (`Set.of(...).contains(null)`), which broke `WorkflowActionPolicyServiceTest`.
  - **Still not done:** the two items that stay with the owner (D7 delegation, D8 access requests); no template of the Permission Holders type exists on DEV yet (the button says so); the counts are not persisted; two-person real flows.

## MODULE-NAMES-ARE-MENU-NAMES-2026-11-18 — a module is shown by the name of its menu, never by its key

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-18, after seeing "app-settings", "audit-trail", "backup", "DOCUMENT_CONTROL", "documents", "training" in the Module filters of Shared Permission Sets, Browse Catalog, Workflow Role Catalog and Policy Resolver; "check everything, there are more hidden"):** every screen that shows a module shows the name of the menu it is under: Document Control, Training Management, Reports, Audit Trail, Backup & Restore, Security & Authorization, Application Settings, System Administration, Notifications, Self-Service, Dashboard, Preferences, Knowledge Base, Controlled Copies, Access Review. Display only: a filter, a form or a URL keeps sending the key.
- **Done.** One function for the screens (`utils/moduleDisplay.ts`: `moduleDisplayName`, `moduleSelectOptions`, `canonicalModuleKey`) and one for the server (`com.eqms.util.ModuleKeys`: `canonical`, `same`, `compactSpellingsOf`; the two lists are kept alike). Applied to Shared Permission Sets (filter), Browse Catalog (filter and column), the permission explorer (module navigation, permission tooltip), Access Profile drawer, workflow tab and role wizard, Workflow Authorization (policy form and Policy Resolver selects; the policy response `moduleLabel` of the server), Workflow Role Catalog (filter, column, form), Access Explorer (filter, column, details), electronic-signature meanings, object numbering, the notification administration (module labels; Security is now Security & Authorization), the notification variable palette, the notification inbox (column and the three module filters, which listed Deviation, CAPA and Change Control that have no notifications; they now list the modules that send them) and the module line of the audit timeline. The Audit Trail already showed the menu name from the server (`AuditMenuModule`) and was left as it is; `formatAuditModuleLabel` is also used for object types and was not changed.
- **The same module spelled several ways in the data** ("report" and "reports", "settings" and "app-settings", "security" and "security-authorization" in permissions; DOCUMENT_CONTROL, Document, documents in notifications) used to give two entries in a filter and a filter that missed the rows of the other spelling. Now the lists show each module once (the server returns one canonical key: Shared Permission Sets, Access Explorer, Browse Catalog) and a filter on one spelling finds all of them (Reports finds 21 permissions = 19 + 2, Application Settings 50 = 27 + 23; the notification inbox compares the column without case, spaces, dots, dashes and underscores). A key that is not named yet is shown readable ("Some New Module") and stays a single option.
- **Data text.** V817 rewrites the generated descriptions of the 12 system permission sets ("Full permissions currently cataloged for the app-settings module." became "...the Application Settings module."), only where the generated words are still there; no key, code or permission changed.
- **Evidence.** `moduleDisplay.test.ts`, `ModuleKeysTest`; the Playwright screenshots of the four screens of the owner and of Access Explorer on DEV; API checks of the lists and of the filters. **Found and fixed on the way:** the Engine Health "since" used the browser's date formatting (`dateFormatGuard.test.ts` caught it): it now uses `formatDateTime`.
- **Not changed on purpose:** the keys stored in the tables, the permission codes (`documents.module.view`...), the workflow module keys of the policies (REPORT, TRAINING, DOCUMENT_CONTROL, SECURITY_REVIEW: only their labels), and the words of audit entries already written.
