## BACKEND-TEST-FAILURES-2026-11-18 — the seven failures the backend suite carried are fixed (cause by cause)

- **Context.** Every full run of the backend suite ended with 6 failures that were identical on a clean checkout of HEAD, plus one that came and went, and one test that never ended and was excluded. Each was traced to its cause; none was a defect of the changes of this period.
- **A permission set that lost its Knowledge access (owner decision 2026-11-18: "grant the permission to the set").** "Document Control - View Everything" (`PS_DOCUMENT_VIEW_ALL`) held `documents.module.view` without `self_service.knowledge.view`, so its people could not open Knowledge (V459 had granted it to every such set at the time). **V818** repeats the V459 grant (adds a missing row only; nothing is removed or changed); applied on DEV. This is a change of the permissions of a set: it was asked and decided, not done by the migration author alone.
- **Tests that depended on the data of the shared dev database, now independent of it:** `UpgradeRevisionIntegrationTest` (chooses a document with exactly one Approver, the floor of the product); `DocumentObsoleteUpgradeProductionPathConcurrencyTest` (the clean-up tries each fixture on its own and leaves what the lifecycle refuses); `ControlledCopyRecallByScanIntegrationTest` (does not pick copies issued for reading or claimed by an open draft, and makes the Distributed paper copies it needs when the database has none left, instead of skipping: a recall is permanent, so each run used them up).
- **A test that asserted a rule that is no longer the rule.** The same test said a person who is not the recipient must not see a Record; the approved rule (DC-VISIBILITY-TIERS-2026-11-07, in `ExecutedRecordService`) is that whoever holds the Records permission sees all of them. The test now asserts the approved rule and that a person without the permission is refused.
- **Tests that counted the whole audit table** (`DocumentObsoleteCascadeAndPreconditionTest`, `DocumentObsoleteNotificationTest`): the table is also written by the running application and by other tests, so "no audit row was created" failed without any row of the document. They count the rows of the document.
- **A real race, in the environment.** The e-mail outbox is claimed row by row (`for update skip locked`) by every worker that shares the database: the backend running on the machine (real mail server) and the worker of the test (whose provider refuses) compete for the same row. When the running backend wins, there is no failure to record. The two tests say so and skip that run (assumption with the reason) instead of failing at random; with the running backend stopped, `DocumentObsoleteNotificationTest` passes 10 of 10 with nothing skipped. **Consequence to know:** while the backend runs on the same database, `tcDoc066` and `tcDoc067` may be skipped.
- **A test that could not finish (`DocumentListVisibilityParityIntegrationTest`).** It asked the detail rule about every document and every revision for every user, and read every list to its end; each list item is built with the workflow actions of the person, so on a database with thousands of records it never ended. It keeps both directions of the parity (everything in a list is allowed by the detail rule; a sample of the records outside it is refused) on an even sample of users and a bounded number of pages.
- **Result.** Full suite: 3042 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 64 skipped (conditional on the data or the environment), `BUILD SUCCESS`.

## ENGINE-HEALTH-TAB-REMOVED-2026-11-19 — the Engine Health tab of Engine Diagnostics is removed from the screen (the backend is kept)

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19: "bỏ luôn toàn bộ Engine Health đi", after asking what the tab is for and being told it is the gate for flipping a resource type from its old checks to the engine).** The fourth tab of Engine Diagnostics (Policy Resolver, Simulator, Relation Definitions, **Engine Health**) is gone. The tab compared, per resource type, the new Hybrid engine with the old evaluator and counted the disagreements ("mismatches") that had to be zero before a cutover flag was switched on, and showed how many real steps the engine decided since the backend started.
- **Why it was no longer needed (found in the code on 2026-11-19, which also corrects what was said earlier in the same conversation).** 20 resource types are decided by the engine alone, with the old checks deleted (`ShadowGuard.ENGINE_DECIDES`: Uncontrolled Copy, Access Review and Audit Review campaigns, Report schedule / run / file, every Training type); for them there is nothing left to compare, so no shadow row is written. REVISION is cut over and its rows are a frozen history. Only **DOCUMENT, CONTROLLED_COPY and CONTROLLED_COPY_BATCH** still run the old checks beside the engine. The shadow table writes a row only when the two disagree (including a disagreement of the reason code only), so the large "total" on the screen was a count of such rows and the tab could not say "N steps were compared, 0 disagreed". The counter of real steps (`EngineEvaluationCounters`) lives in memory and starts again at zero when the backend restarts.
- **Option offered and not taken.** A persisted "compared N times" counter for the three types still compared (a new table, a change in `DocumentService` and `ShadowGuard`, a Decision Log entry and tests). The owner asked first what the tab was for and then chose to remove it entirely; nothing of that counter was built.
- **Done (frontend only).** `EngineHealthTab.tsx` deleted; the tab entry, its label and its `?tab=engineHealth` value removed from `AuthorizationDiagnosticsView.tsx`; from `services/api/security.ts` the calls `getAuthorizationShadowMismatchSummary`, `getAuthorizationShadowMismatchesPaged`, `getEngineEvaluationCounts` and the type `AuthorizationShadowMismatch`, which nothing else used. GitNexus: `EngineHealthTab` had one caller (the diagnostics view), risk LOW. `tsc` clean, the Security & Authorization tests (82) pass, and the tab is absent on DEV (the page lists Policy Resolver, Simulator, Relation Definitions).
- **Not changed on purpose (backend).** The endpoints (`/security/authorization-shadow-mismatches/summary` and `/paged`, `/authorization/engine-evaluation-counts`), `AuthorizationShadowEvaluationService`, the table `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events` and the recording for the three types are unchanged and keep working; no migration, no data deleted.
- **Consequence the owner should know.** There is now **no screen** to see disagreements between the engine and the old checks for DOCUMENT, CONTROLLED_COPY and CONTROLLED_COPY_BATCH; they can still be read from the table `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events` (a disagreement of allow / deny is `policy_allowed <> legacy_allowed`). Before any of these three is cut over, that table has to be checked by hand or a new view is needed. Older entries of this log that say Engine Health shows something (2026-11-18: phases A and B, the Training adapters, the Report workflow, the three small items) describe what was true on that date.
- **Not done.** `docs/SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_USER_GUIDE.md` and the other documents still mention Engine Health and have not been updated. Open for the owner: when to cut over the three remaining types, after which the shadow code, the endpoints and the counters can be removed and this entry closed.

## INTEGRATIONS-TABS-2026-11-19 — the three tabs of Configuration > Integrations, with the data they needed (V819)

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19: "làm ngay, từ FE, BE, API, DB"), after the wireframes of the three tabs were chosen.** All 15 proposals were built (S1–S5, U1–U5, O1–O5). Change record: `docs/validation/INTEGRATIONS_TABS_2026_11_19_CHANGE_RECORD.md`.
- **Done.** V819 (the last warning and the last test of each integration), `IntegrationCheckService`, `OnlyOfficeConnectionCheckService` (health check and the saved JWT secret on the command service), `GET /settings/system/integrations/checks`, `jwtSecretChangedAt` kept by the server, the redesigned tabs, the retention acknowledgement before Save.
- **Not done on purpose: a fingerprint of the JWT secret.** The wireframe showed "••••a3f9". Showing even a few characters of a hash of an administrator-chosen secret lets someone who sees the screen test guesses against it, so it was left out until the owner decides. The card shows only "A secret is saved" and when it last changed. **Open for the owner/QA: show a fingerprint or not.**
- **Open: the unit of the budget.** During this work the label of the field was changed to **"Budget (GB)"** in `StorageUsagePanel.tsx`, but the screen still converts with 1024³ and shows sizes as GiB/MiB, so "1" means 1 GiB (1.0737 GB). Either the label goes back to GiB or the arithmetic and the displayed units become decimal; the stored value is bytes in both cases. **Not changed by the assistant; needs the owner's choice.**
- **Open: the retention acknowledgement is a screen rule only.** The API accepts a change of the retention without it. If QA wants it enforced, it needs a server-side contract (a confirmation token or the electronic signature the other irreversible actions use).
- **Open: no enforced consequence text for "OnlyOffice is off".** The screen says only "the OnlyOffice editor and viewer cannot be opened", which the code confirms for the editor, the stored-file viewer and the Training editor (`buildEditorConfig`, `buildStoredFileViewConfig`, `buildTrainingEditConfig` all refuse when it is off); what the rest of the application does then (previews of other kinds) was not checked.
- **Known limits.** Notices sent before V819 have no recorded time (the screen says "No warning is recorded"); a real refusal of the JWT secret (command-service code 6) is covered by a test against a fake server but was not produced on DEV; the callback of the Document Server into this application is not tested and not claimed.
- **Missing documents.** `CLAUDE.md` names `docs/system/`, `09_ASSURANCE_OPERATING_MODEL.md` and `08_ONE_CHANGE_RECORD_TEMPLATE.md`; none of them exists in the repository, so the evidence conventions could not be read and the record follows the precedent of 2026-11-18.

## EFFECTIVE-ACCESS-TAB-REMOVED-2026-11-19 — the Effective Access tab of Access Profile Details is removed from the screen (the backend is kept); two other tabs of the same screen corrected

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19: "bỏ", after asking whether the tab could go).** The tab is removed from Access Profile Details.
- **Why.** Found on DEV on 2026-11-19: the tab evaluated only the Document Control lifecycle (`AccessEffectiveService` walks `RevisionWorkflowAction` and the Cancel/Obsolete capabilities of documents). The database also holds workflow action policies for TRAINING (86), REPORT (17) and SECURITY_REVIEW (6), and workflow roles for training (2), so a profile that works only in Training showed "0 allowed". Of the 128 rows shown for a profile, 110 were "this action does not exist for that status" (`NO_MATCHING_POLICY`) counted under "Not allowed"; the owner could not read the tab. The permissions, shared sets and workflow roles of a profile are shown completely on the Permissions, Shared Permission Sets and Workflow Eligibility tabs.
- **Options offered and not taken.** (A) keep the tab and list, for every module that has workflow policies, each action with the permission it needs and whether the profile holds it; (B) connect the real evaluators of Training, Reports and Security Review so the result is per status like Document Control. B changes authorization logic and needs a QA/Security decision on what each result means.
- **Done (frontend).** `EffectiveAccessPanel.tsx`, `EffectiveActionRow.tsx`, `AccessProfileEffectiveAccessTab.tsx` and the panel's test deleted; the tab entry and its render removed from `AccessProfileDetailView.tsx`; `getEffectiveAccess` and its three types removed from `services/api/security.ts`.
- **Not changed on purpose (backend).** `GET /security/access-profiles/{id}/effective-access` and `AccessEffectiveService` are kept; nothing on the screen calls them now. During this work the query response was extended (outcome buckets `denied` / `needsAssignment` / `notApplicable`, the granted `modules`, readable action labels; 12 tests in `AccessEffectiveServiceTest`) and is not used by any screen. The per-user diagnosis (`EffectiveAccessDiagnosisController`, Access Explorer) is untouched; it was not compared with this tab.
- **Consequence the owner should know.** There is no screen now that shows how the Document Control lifecycle actions resolve for a whole Access Profile; for one person, use the per-user diagnosis.
- **Also corrected in the same change (frontend + one backend parameter).** Workflow Eligibility: the "Users" column showed the users of the role across the whole system (5, 1, 7) while the profile's own Assigned Users was 0; it now shows the users who hold the role through this profile, and the server's "users" sort follows the same number (`countForProfileId`; `WorkflowRoleServiceUsersTest`). Shared Permission Sets: a Status column (Active / Inactive) was added.
- **Not done.** `docs/SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_USER_GUIDE.md` (section 2.3) is updated; `SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_DOCUMENTS_UAT_GUIDE_VI.md` (lines about "View Effective Access") and `EQMS_SECURITY_AUTHORIZATION_AND_DOCUMENTS_TEST_GUIDE.md` (the Effective Access steps and evidence lines) still tell the tester to open a tab that no longer exists, and `docs/as-is-sds/06-role-authorization-model.md` still describes it; they need the owner's decision on how those UAT checks are done now.

## ACCESS-PROFILE-MODULE-COVERAGE-2026-11-19 — module coverage of Access Profile screens, the Action Matrix module filter, and the Shared Permission Sets list

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19: "kiểm tra toàn bộ Access Profile, xem đã thiết kế cho toàn bộ tất cả module trong dự án chưa … khắc phục ngay"; "thêm trường lọc theo Module" in the Action Matrix).** Display and listing changes only; no permission, policy or lifecycle rule is changed.
- **Found (DEV, 2026-11-19).** The permission catalog stores 15 module keys for 12 menus (`app-settings` and `settings`, `report` and `reports`, `security` and `security-authorization`, ...). The permission explorer (Access Profile Permissions tab, Permission Set forms, Role Setup Wizard, Lifecycle Policy form) made one pill per key, so "Application Settings" appeared twice (23 and 26 permissions). The Shared Permission Sets table named modules by key ("audit-trail · backup · +10 more"). Workflow roles exist only for documents (5) and training (2); workflow action policies exist for DOCUMENT_CONTROL, TRAINING, REPORT and SECURITY_REVIEW, and those of Reports and Security Review have only PERMISSION actors, so for them the permission alone decides. Document Control visibility is a Document Control concept by design.
- **Done (frontend).** The explorer merges the keys that share a menu name (one pill per menu; the totals are unchanged: 139 granted on the Admin profile); the sets table names modules by menu; the Workflow Eligibility tab says which modules have roles and that the others follow permissions; the Action Matrix has a Module filter, and the Object Type list holds only the object types of that module (Document Control, Training Management, Reports, Access Review & Audit Trail Review).
- **Done (backend, earlier the same day).** In the list of one Access Profile's permission sets, a managed (`ROLE_`) set of another profile that is attached to it is listed (it grants permissions through the profile); the profile's own managed set stays on the Permissions tab (`PermissionSetService.isListed`, `PermissionSetServiceListedTest`). Found on DEV: profile DCO Test holds `ROLE_DCO` (147 permissions, "System-managed compatibility set") that no screen listed.
- **Not changed on purpose.** The module keys stored in the permission catalog (the merge is on the screen only); the Edit mode of the Shared Permission Sets tab still lists no `ROLE_` set, so an attached `ROLE_` set of another profile cannot be unticked there.
- **Open for the owner.** Whether `ROLE_DCO` on DEV is intended or old data; whether the duplicate module keys should be unified in the database (a migration of regulated catalog data).

## TRANSITION-POLICY-FORM-LAYOUTS-2026-11-19 — four ways to fill in a Transition Policy, and the Action Matrix lists only objects with a status stepper

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19).** (1) "Action Matrix chỉ những cái nào có stepper quản lý trạng thái workflow mới áp dụng … loại bỏ bớt những cái không có". (2) "Về phần New / Edit Policy, bản chất thay đổi chỉ là giao diện … thiết kế 1 TabNav để user có thể switch giữa 4 chế độ", after four wireframes (`wireframe4.html`: sentence, steps, matrix, templates) were proposed.
- **Action Matrix.** An object is listed only if its own screen shows a status stepper that the server defines (`WorkflowProgressService`, `ReportRevisionWorkflow`, `TrainingWorkflows`): Document, Document Revision, Controlled Copy (with its batch), Uncontrolled Copy, Report Template, and the Training Requirement, Matrix Version, Activity, Assessment, Job Assignment and Personnel Review. Removed from the matrix: Report Schedule, Report Run, Report File, Training Participant, Record Export (and its batch), Trainer Exception, Suspension, Vendor, Record, Access Review Campaign and Audit Trail Review Campaign. Their policies are not deleted or changed; they stay on the Transition Policies tab. Consequence: the module "Access Review & Audit Trail Review" no longer appears in the matrix.
- **Form.** New / Edit Policy has a TabNav (Sentence, Steps, Matrix, Templates) over one form state: the layouts only arrange the fields, validation, preview and save are unchanged (`PolicyRuleLayouts.tsx`, `PolicyRuleLayoutProps`). The choice is kept in this browser. Document Type, Priority, Active, Description and Change Reason moved into an "Options" block; the Required Permission and Actors blocks are the same components as before. The layouts warn when another active policy exists for the same object, action, status and document type (the list of policies is loaded with the form); the warning does not block saving.
- **Not done on purpose.** The "who can do this now" count of wireframe D (it needs a backend preview API and a decision on what counts as "can"); the Capability (state) policy form keeps its old layout; the policy duplicate screen is unchanged.
- **Templates are labels, not new rules.** "Anyone with the permission", "Only the owner", "Only the author", "The assigned reviewer / approver" and "The recipient" write the same actor types the form already wrote (`PERMISSION`, `OWNER`, `AUTHOR`, `ASSIGNED_REVIEWER`, `ASSIGNED_APPROVER`, `RECIPIENT`), offered only when the action allows that type. QA should confirm the wording.

## OBJECT-RULE-AND-EXCLUSION-RULES-ALL-MODULES-2026-11-19 — scope of the next design, and a signature fix

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19, chosen from options).** (1) Object Access Rules extend beyond Document Control to Training Management, Reports and Security Reviews (Access Review, Audit Trail Review). (2) A module with no Object Rule keeps today's behaviour (open). (3) New exclusion rules ("a different person") for Access Review and Audit Trail Review are OFF by default; an admin turns them on with a reason and a signature. (4) The unchecked signature on the Document Revision Integrity rules is fixed now, as its own change.
- **Why.** Object Access Rules are Document-only in code (`ObjectResourceType`, `ObjectAccessAction`, `ObjectAccessEvaluationService` are called only for Document, Revision and Controlled Copy). Access Review and Audit Trail Review policies (V814) carry no relations, so no "a different person" rule can be set for them. Plan: `docs/plans/KE_HOACH_OBJECT_RULE_VA_LUAT_LOAI_TRU_TOAN_MODULE_2026_11_19.md`.
- **Done (this change).** `UserManagementService.updateDocumentAdministration` now checks the electronic signature before any state change (wrong owner, expired or missing token is refused with nothing changed), records the signature (`SOD_RULE_CHANGE`, entity `DOCUMENT_WORKFLOW_SETTING`) and links it to the audit entry. Before, the screen collected a signature and the server ignored it. Tests: `UserManagementServiceDocumentRulesSignatureTest` (unsigned refused, other person's token refused, signed save changes the rules and links the signature).
- **Not done on purpose.** The extension of Object Rules and the new exclusion rules are design only. Not decided yet: the single-admin organisation (exception with a reason and a second signature, or none), the link to Training's `training.scope.all` and to the open Report visibility decision, and whether the exclusion rules are mandatory or removable.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19, later the same day: "admin có toàn quyền").** The exclusion rules are configurable by the admin: none is mandatory or fixed, and an organisation with a single admin is not blocked from turning a rule off. Read as authority over the CONFIGURATION (turn a rule on or off with a reason and a signature, every change audited). It is NOT read as a runtime bypass: while a rule is on, it applies to the admin like any other person. If the owner meant a runtime bypass (the admin may act against a rule that is on), that needs its own named decision, because it would defeat the rule.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19, final reading: "vượt ở đây là admin được toàn quyền bật tắt các cấu hình").** "Override" means the admin has full authority over the CONFIGURATION: turning any rule on or off. There is NO runtime override: no permission, no override screen, no OVERRIDE entry on an audit record. The admin is also an ordinary GMP employee and has a separate, normal account for operational work; the exclusion rules apply to that account like to anyone else. This closes the reading "the admin may act against a rule that is on", which was recorded in error earlier on the same day and is withdrawn.
- **Consequence to keep in view.** Nothing in the code stops a person from doing operational GMP work (review, approve) with the admin account; the separation depends on the process (two accounts) and on the permission rules. If the owner wants it enforced, a Permission Conflict Rule can pair the admin permissions with the review/approve permissions; not decided.
- **Not checked.** The save was not tried through the running application (no DEV password in the session); the unit tests use mocks. Rule changes already made before this fix carry no signature.

## SEPARATION-RULES-AND-OBJECT-RULES-BUILT-2026-11-19 — what was built from the 2026-11-19 plan, and what it changed

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-19: "làm hết 4 giai đoạn", then "tách thành các tab theo module, UI giống tab Document Revision Integrity").** Phase 1 (review-campaign rules), phase 2 (a screen to switch the rules), phase 3 (Object Access Rules beyond Document Control: Training Management first) and phase 4 (Reports, Security Reviews) are built together, because phase 3's design covers every module that has a policy-backed adapter. The Separation Rules screen is one tab per module, laid out like Document Revision Integrity (tick boxes, Edit / Cancel / Reset / Save in the header). Transition Policy Details and Edit Transition Policy are two screens.
- **Built (backend).** V820: relations `CAMPAIGN_CREATOR`, `ITEM_SUBJECT` (Access Review), `ENTRY_PERFORMER` (Audit Trail Review); the campaign adapters resolve them (the id given may be a campaign or one of its items) and the item decision is asked of the engine on the item as well. V821: `separation_rule_catalog` (every existing forbidden relation of Report and Training, plus the four new review rules, which start OFF). `SeparationRuleService` + `/authorization/separation-rules` (list with server-side filters; save of several rules under ONE signature, all or nothing, one audit entry per policy; a per-rule save). `SeparationRuleSwitch`: the Training lists of people who can be chosen for a step follow the switch (before, they were fixed in code). V822: `module_key` and `object_type` on `object_access_rules` (every old row is Document Control); `ModuleObjectRuleService` (same reading as Document Control, scoped to module and object type; no rule = no limit; an ALLOW rule makes the rest a refusal) read by the engine as one more step after the object-scope step, for the adapters of Training, Reports (incl. report templates) and Security Reviews; Document Control never reads these rules and they never read Document Control rules. Options API gives the modules, object types, actions and statuses from the adapters and policies (nothing typed twice).
- **Defect found and fixed.** `WorkflowActionPolicyService.setForbiddenRelations` and `setPolicyRelations` removed relation rows by query while the policy kept them in its cascade/orphan-removal collection, so the flush put them back: a forbidden or required relation could never be removed, and setting one again returned 500. They now change the policy's own collection. Found by the live test of the new rules; 8 regression checks on real policies pass.
- **Impact analysis (GitNexus).** `AuthorizationEngineService.authorize`: HIGH (70 symbols reach it); the change is one added step that runs only when an adapter takes part (Training, Report, Security Review) and a rule exists, and returns `OUT_OF_SCOPE`; without rules nothing changes (live tests of Document Control and of the existing flows pass). `TrainingActivityService.keptApart`: MEDIUM (17), behaviour unchanged while the rules are ON, which they are today. Other edited symbols: LOW.
- **Evidence.** `eqms-backend/docs/validation/evidence/separation_rules_2026_11_19/` (live scripts and README with results: 16, 22, 15, 3, 30 and 8 checks, all passing on DEV) and the unit tests named there. Backend suites related to authorization, workflow policies, Training, Reports and Object Rules pass; the only failures in the wider run are the 5 tests of `TrainingAdministrationPermissionTest`, which need an outbox encryption key that the test configuration does not set (not related to this change).
- **Not done / open.** (1) The DEPARTMENT dimension of a Training rule and Reports/Training objects with a real rule were not tried live (no suitable records on DEV): unit tests only. (2) A rule limits ACTIONS decided by the engine; lists and views of a module keep their own scope. (3) Document Revision Integrity keeps its six fixed switches; it is not part of the catalog. (4) Rules a person adds by hand in the policy form are not in the catalog and so do not appear on the Separation Rules tabs. (5) Two administrators saving rules of the same policy at the same moment are serialised by a row lock, not tested under load. (6) Nothing is committed; the DEV password was used only through an environment variable.

## SECURITY-MODULE-CLEANUP-2026-11-20 — unused endpoints, data and one menu entry removed; one defect fixed

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20: "làm cả luôn cho đến khi hoàn thiện"; "bảng shadow 348 nghìn dòng, hãy xoá đi"; aim: the software is easy to use, too much redundant information confuses users).** Remove what nothing uses in Security & Authorization, after an impact analysis (GitNexus re-indexed the same day; its transitive counts are inflated by class-level edges, so the decisions rest on direct callers, a search of main and test code, and the Administrative Operation Catalog).
- **Removed (backend).** The three Engine Health read endpoints (`/security/authorization-shadow-mismatches*`) and `/authorization/engine-evaluation-counts`; the Effective Access Diagnosis controller and service (`/security/effective-access/diagnose`, replaced by the Simulator); the Participant Reconciliation controller and service; `PUT /settings/users/{id}/role`; on Access Profile the single-change endpoints (`PUT/POST/DELETE permission-sets`, `PUT/POST/DELETE workflow-roles`, `POST managed-permissions`) and the non-paged `GET {id}/users`: every screen saves a profile with `PUT {id}/configuration` or creates it with `POST /full`, and assigns one user with `POST/DELETE users/{id}`, which stay. Their entries in `AdministrativeOperationCatalog` are gone; the three tests of the removed service methods were moved to the surviving save path, with the same protections (no self-grant of a critical permission set, nothing changes when refused).
- **Removed (data).** V823: all 348,491 rows of `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events` (none was an allow/deny disagreement; they differ only in the wording of the reason; nothing written since 2026-08-11) and the table `security_legacy_role_mapping_exceptions` (0 rows, no reader). The shadow table itself stays: ShadowGuard still writes a row if the engine and the old check of Document, Controlled Copy or Controlled Copy Batch ever differ.
- **Removed (frontend).** About twenty API functions nobody calls and the interfaces that only they used.
- **Menu.** "Workflow Role Catalog" is no longer a menu entry; it is the fourth tab "Workflow Roles" of Workflow Authorization (same list, same New Custom Role form). The old URLs redirect.
- **Defect found and fixed on the way.** Deleting an Access Profile answered 500 (Hibernate refused the flush: loaded entities still pointed at the role). The links are now removed first, the signature and the audit entry are recorded while the role still exists, and the role is removed by id after the session is cleared. Verified live (204, audit entry with signature).
- **Not done on purpose, with the reason.** (1) The cutover of Document, Controlled Copy and Controlled Copy Batch to the engine, and with it the removal of `ShadowGuard` and of `EngineEvaluationCounters`: the evidence (no disagreement) comes from DEV traffic only, and the step changes who may act on regulated records, so it needs the owner's explicit decision and evidence from real traffic. (2) Removing the legacy `actors` of a policy: `ControlledCopyAuthorizationService` and `AccessEffectiveService` still read them. (3) Folding the six Document Revision Integrity switches into the engine: separate change, the department rule is not a relation.
- **Evidence.** `eqms-backend/docs/validation/evidence/separation_rules_2026_11_19/wave1_live.py` (33 checks: 15 removed endpoints answer 404/405, the kept ones answer, the profile create / configuration save / stale save / assign / unassign / delete work, the shadow table is empty, the legacy table is gone, navigation has no role-catalog entry). Backend: 429 tests of authorization, workflow, profile, navigation, operation catalog, knowledge share, user and report areas pass. Frontend: tsc clean; the two failing tests of `UserProfileView.test.tsx` belong to another session's uncommitted change of that screen.

## DASHBOARD-WIDGET-LIBRARY-2026-11-20 — a widget library on the Dashboard, and Backup Status Overview moved into it

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Một kho card/widget có thể hiển thị trên Dashboard. Khi nào user muốn thêm gì thì có thể thêm tự do. Các chức năng di chuyển, thay đổi vị trí, kích cỡ vẫn giữ nguyên" and "bỏ màn hình Backup Status Overview, gộp hết nó vào Dashboard chính".
- **Library.** The Customize Dashboard drawer became the Widget Library: every card the person may use, by category, with a search; Add puts the card at the end of the dashboard, Remove takes it off. Moving and Half/Full are unchanged. "Add Widgets" opens the library straight from the page. A card marked `defaultVisible: false` is in the library until added and is not brought onto a dashboard whose saved layout predates it.
- **Who sees which card.** Authorisation is unchanged: a card is offered only when the caller's own dashboard builds it (administrators' cards need `dashboard.admin.view`; the backup cards need `backup.module.view`, the permission the server asks for on every backup read). A layout id never grants access. The administrator's library also holds the cards about the administrator's own work (my tasks, my documents, document activity, workflow pipeline), which were not available to them before.
- **Backup cards.** Nine cards replace the screen: Last successful backup, Failed backups, Needs attention, Backup runs (30 days) are on by default for those who may view Backup & Restore; Completed backups, Backups without a passing check, Backup ledger, File store check (with Check Now for `backup.backup.verify`) and Manual copy are in the library. One shared reading of the status serves all shown cards (every 30 s while the tab is visible), and none is made while no backup card is shown. A failed read shows a retry or "the last values read", never zeros.
- **Removed.** The menu item (front end and `NavigationService`), the route and `BackupOverviewView`. `/backup-restore/overview` and `/backup-restore/dr-drills` now go to `/dashboard`; `/backup-restore` goes to Backup History.
- **Server.** The layout validator accepts the nine new ids and up to 80 cards (was 30; the catalogue has 35). Role-default layouts use the same validator.
- **Open, for the owner.** (1) The audit object type of the file store check is still "Backup Status Overview" (`AuditObjectRegistry`); renaming it would split the audit history, so it was left. (2) A person with a saved layout gets the four default backup cards once; if they hide them and save, that is kept. (3) UAT guides and SDS-BR-001 sections that describe the screen were not rewritten.

## DASHBOARD-WIDGET-LIBRARY-MODULES-2026-11-20 — the library as one accordion section per module, and ten more cards

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Thiết kế thành dạng Accordion, mỗi accordion là 1 module. Khi click sẽ show các widget liên quan. Thêm nhiều widget hơn cho tôi." This replaces the category tabs of DASHBOARD-WIDGET-LIBRARY-2026-11-20.
- **Library.** One section per module, named as its menu is (Document Control, Training Management, Reports, Audit Trail, Security & Authorization, System Administration, Backup & Restore, Notifications), closed until clicked, each showing "n of m added". A search opens every module with a match and hides the others.
- **Ten new cards, all in the library only (none appears on a dashboard until added).** Real reads only: Unread notifications and Recent notifications (the person's own); My training tasks and Training signatures asked of me (`/training/me/counts`, shown for `training.self.view`); Recent report runs and Failed report runs (the run list the Report Run History screen uses, shown for `report.module.view`; Failed counts Failed and Dead-lettered); Recent audit events (`/dashboard/recent-activity`, shown for `dashboard.admin.view`); Open access reviews, Open audit trail reviews and Open controlled copy discrepancies (from the system-monitoring read, administrators).
- **Placement by module.** The existing cards moved to the module of their menu: the document and workflow cards under Document Control; users, sessions, logins, signatures and compliance queues under Security & Authorization; uptime, database and runtime under System Administration. The "pending" cards are listed under Document Control because their figures count document revisions.
- **Server.** The layout validator accepts the ten new ids (45 in all, limit 80). Authorisation is unchanged: a card is offered by the dashboard that builds it, and the server checks the permission again on every read.
- **Not done.** No card for Training Management beyond the person's own counts, and none for Notifications administration; a card needs a read that exists and a permission that fits, and those two were not found. Say which you want next.

## DASHBOARD-WIDGET-LIBRARY-TRAINING-NOTIFICATIONS-2026-11-20 — nine more cards (Training Management, Notifications), GSAP accordion, shared Button

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Thêm card cho Training và Notifications nữa. Dùng GSAP cho animation đóng/mở accordion. Các button thiết kế sử dụng `components/ui/button/`."
- **Notifications (3).** Personal notifications (personal against system, from the notification summary); Notifications by type (the person's own kinds, most frequent first, none with zero); Failed notification deliveries (the server total of the delivery-failure list, shown only for `settings.notification_policy.manage`, the permission the server asks for on that list).
- **Training Management (6).** My trainings to do, Training signatures asked of me (list) and My recent training records (the person's own, `training.self.view`; a voided record is marked Voided); Training activities in progress, Overdue training activities and Trainings awaiting QA confirmation (the server's own total for the filter, shown for `training.activity.view`; the server still scopes the list to what the person may see).
- **Server.** The layout validator accepts the nine ids (54 in all, limit 80). All nine start in the library only.
- **Accordion.** The library sections open and close with GSAP (height and opacity, chevron turning; reduced-motion shows the end state at once); a closed section is not in the page. The section headers, Add / Remove and the rows of the new list cards use the shared `Button`.
- **Not done.** The row of a training list opens the person's task list (or records), not the single training: no address for one of the person's trainings was found. The activity cards count by status and by "overdue" only; a count by department or kind needs a decision on which ones matter.

## VISIBILITY-TIERS-AND-OBJECT-RULE-REMOVAL-2026-11-20 — three visibility levels for every module; admin Object Access Rules and Document profile scope to be removed (DESIGN ONLY)

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20, agreed in conversation).** Remove the administrator-created Object Access Rules (all modules) and the Business Unit/Department scope of Access Profiles for Document Control; replace the "who sees a record" part with three levels: in the record's flow (default, no permission), own department (new `<module>.view_department` permission, department = the Department written on the record), all (`view_all`). Remove the system-wide Document visibility mode `ALL_WITH_VIEW_PERMISSION`. Actions keep the four Hybrid layers (permission, relation to the record, state, separation of duties) plus signature/reason; department is not a condition of an action.
- **Document Control specifics.** "In the flow" is the flow of the parent document. When a revision is upgraded and Author/Co-Author/Reviewer/Approver are changed, the people removed no longer see the new revision and keep the older ones. A Controlled Copy recipient keeps seeing the own copy. Download and print stay decided by the Controlled Copies Policy (no new permission). Uncontrolled Copy follows Controlled Copy.
- **Kept.** Knowledge Base keeps its own model, and the table `object_access_rules` stays for Knowledge shares (rows owned by a department or business unit). On DEV the table holds 3 rows, all Knowledge shares, and no administrator-created rule, so removing the administrator path changes no decision on DEV; other environments are not measured.
- **Status: design only.** Nothing is built. Plan, scope, risks and the five open confirmations (copy visibility at the department level, `report_template_role_scopes`, Training flow level, order, other environments) are in `docs/plans/THIET_KE_TAM_NHIN_VA_GO_OBJECT_ACCESS_RULES_2026_11_20.md`. It supersedes section 4.2 and WP1(a)/WP7 of `KE_HOACH_HOAN_THIEN_PHAN_QUYEN_TOAN_MODULE_2026_11_20.md`.
- **Not decided.** Whether the department-level reader sees copies; the Reports template scope; the Training flow level; any retirement of the Document shadow/legacy checks.

## VISIBILITY-TIERS-BUILT-2026-11-20 — what was built from VISIBILITY-TIERS-AND-OBJECT-RULE-REMOVAL-2026-11-20, how it was checked, and what is still open

- **Decisions of the owner while building (2026-11-20, chosen from the offered options; every one was the recommended option).** (1) Copy record for the people in the flow: the **record only** (list, detail, signatures, Audit Trail), not the content (issued PDF, evidence files). (2) "In the flow" of a copy is read **per revision of the copy** (Author, Co-author, Reviewer, Approver of that revision, and the creator of the Document). (3) The department level does **not** open the file of a revision under review (review snapshot). (4) The setting "Retain visibility for removed participants" **stays as an exception** (default off; DEV has Reviewer on, so a removed Reviewer of the Document still sees its newer revisions and copies by that setting). (5) The recipient data of a copy (name, employee code, department, e-mail, signature) is shown in full to the people in the flow. (6) Training Management and Reports get the same kind of picker with two levels each (no new permission). (7) The other modules (Notifications, Self-Service, Audit Trail, Backup & Restore, Security & Authorization incl. Access Review, Application Settings, System Administration, Preferences, System Information): the owner asked for **a design module by module**; nothing is built for them.
- **Built.** V826 (permission `documents.document.view_department` + set `PS_DOCUMENT_VIEW_DEPARTMENT`); the department level in `DocumentAuthorizationService` (detail, Document and Revision list predicates, menu, report SQL `DocumentReportVisibility`), read-only (`canViewDocumentAsActor` excludes it) and indirect (never a direct stakeholder); copies by flow (`canSeeControlledCopyRecord`, `copyFlowPredicate`, Uncontrolled Copy list/detail, report SQL, Audit tab, capabilities hide the content actions with the code `COPY_CONTENT_NOT_VISIBLE`); the picker on the Access Profile and in the Wizard; V827 removes the system-wide mode `documentVisibilityMode` (open mode) and its Document Properties switch; the Access Profile scope (Business Unit / Department) is removed from entity, DTOs, services and screens and V828 drops `roles.business_unit_scope` and `roles.department_scope` (the 11 DEV values are kept in `docs/validation/evidence/visibility_tiers_2026_11_20/`); `ObjectAccessEvaluationService` deleted and the Document Control adapters' object-scope step now only confirms the record exists; `AccessEffectiveService` has no third evaluator; the administrator Object Access Rules (controller, services, DTOs, engine step, adapter hooks, screens, menu, route, permission `security.object_rules.view`) are removed, V829 drops `access_profile_object_rules` and the V822 columns and refuses to run while an administrator-created rule exists. Kept: the table `object_access_rules` for Knowledge shares, the permissions `security.object_rules.manage` and `.share_knowledge_any_department` (legacy names), `report_template_role_scopes`.
- **Checked on DEV after each step.** Unit tests (department level 12, copies by flow 11, visibility base 15, plus the suites touched: authorization, adapters, eligibility, effective access, audit trail, file access, profile security) all green; live scripts against the real API with real accounts and a temporary profile that was deleted afterwards: Document and Revision list totals equal "department U flow" exactly for a QC and a QA user, detail allowed/denied correctly, a user without the grant unchanged (21/21); Controlled Copy list totals equal "recipient U flow" exactly, the flow-only copy opens as a record but its issued PDF, evidence and preview are refused and the capabilities say so, a person without the base copy permission is refused, the view-everything holder unchanged (36/36); revision actions of the current reviewer still allowed, eligible reviewers list answers, effective-access answers; after V829 the removed endpoints answer 404, the 3 Knowledge shares and their list remain (13/13). Screenshots were delivered for each step.
- **Not verified / open.** The per-revision case (a person in an old revision, not in the new one) is covered by unit tests only: DEV has no such pair for the test accounts. Real two-person canary for the department level is not done. The Reports and Training pickers were checked by unit tests and by screen, not by a live two-person run. DEV had no administrator Object Access Rule, no Access Profile with a department level and no environment with the open mode on; **other environments must be checked before release** (V829 refuses to run if a rule exists; V827 removes the open mode key, which makes the people who relied on it fall back to the flow level until the administrator grants a level). Seven `MainLayout` tests and two `UserProfileView` tests fail in the working tree because of other work; they are not caused by this change. The design for the remaining modules is still to be written.

## MODULES-WITHOUT-RECORDS-AND-ROUTE-GATE-TEST-2026-11-20 — no visibility levels for modules without records, Audit Trail unchanged, a test that every route that changes data has a gate

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** The modules that have no states and no department (Notifications, Self-Service, Audit Trail, Backup & Restore, Security & Authorization incl. Access Review, Application Settings, System Administration, Preferences, System Information) get **no visibility levels**: whoever holds the permission may enter, as it is today. Electronic signature and Audit Trail of sensitive changes, the two-person rule of a restore and the Separation Rules stay. The global Audit Trail screen stays "whoever holds `audit.view` sees every record"; the Audit Trail tab of a record keeps following the visibility of that record.
- **Built.** `MutatingRouteAuthorizationGateTest` (source scan, no database): every POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE route of a controller must reach a gate (permission check, authorization engine / shadow guard, electronic-signature token check, `PreAuthorize`) in its body or in the service methods it calls, two levels deep; a route that is safe for a reason the scan cannot see is listed with the reason (3 today: the two Revision workspace batch routes, which gate each item in `RevisionService`, and the Report schedule owner change, which runs inside the shadow guard); an exception that is no longer needed must leave the list. The test passes on the current code and fails when an open route is added (checked with a temporary controller, removed afterwards).
- **Limit.** The scan reads text: it proves a gate exists on the path, not that the gate is the right one. It does not replace the per-module negative tests.

## SECURITY-AUTHORIZATION-COMPLETION-CLEANUP-2026-11-20 — what no longer had a user was removed after the visibility-tier changes

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Complete the Security & Authorization functions according to the requirements already built, and remove what is no longer used."
- **Found by reading the source, not by guessing.** Method: for every method of the security services and controllers, the number of references in main and test code; for every permission of the catalog, the references in backend, frontend and the workflow/lifecycle policies; for every frontend export and api function of the security area, the imports; for every backend route of the security controllers, a caller in the frontend; for every frontend call, a backend route. A hit was kept when a reason for it existed (a scheduled job, a route the frontend builds dynamically).
- **Removed (backend).** `AccessProfileService.validateProfileAssignment` (user creation has its own combination check); `AuthorizationEngineService.relationDefinitionsFor`; in `ControlledCopyAuthorizationService` the methods `diagnoseCopyAction`, `diagnoseBatchAction`, `requireEvidenceReadAccess`, `requireExpireControlledCopy`, `requireUploadEvidence`, `requireDownloadAccess`, `requireTokenDownloadAccess` (the preview and download links are decided by the link token and the preview grant; a test that stubbed `requireTokenDownloadAccess` no longer does); in `DocumentAuthorizationService` the methods `canPerformDocumentMasterLifecycleAction`, `requireCanEditRevisionFileOnline`, `requireCanManageDocumentWorkspace`, `requireCanPublishRevision`; in `TrainingAuthorizationService` six methods nobody called (`requireCanManageDocumentTrainingPlan`, `requireCanManageTrainingCatalog`, `requireCanViewTrainingModule` and their three `can...` forms); `WorkflowActionPolicyService.isActorTypeAllowed` (its five tests moved to `WorkflowRegistryActorTypesTest`, which tests the registry directly); the route `GET /security/workflow-action-policies/defaults/document-revision` and its service method (no screen called it); the legacy route `GET /settings/users/export` (a redirect nobody called) with `UserManagementService.writeUsersExport`; the route `GET /security/access-profiles/{id}/effective-access` with `AccessEffectiveService`, its three response types and its test (no screen called it since the panel was removed; the user-level Access Explorer is a different function and stays).
- **Removed (frontend).** the type `PolicyEditorMode`, the component `PermissionResourceGroup`, the api functions `getOnlyOfficeConfiguration`, `updateSecurityConfiguration`, `exportUsers`. No frontend call to a missing backend route exists.
- **Removed (data).** V830 deletes six permissions that nothing reads (`settings.dictionary.view/manage`, `documents.form.configure/design_efield/fill_eform`, `training.audit.view`) and their rows in permission sets.
- **Kept on purpose, with the reason.** `WorkflowActionDefaultPolicyRegistry.allTraining` (used by the Training adapter tests as the source of real policies); the Access Review reminder job (scheduled); `SectionCard` and `removeAccents` in the user profile screens (other work in progress there); the backend routes behind `getOnlyOfficeConfiguration` and `updateSecurityConfiguration` (System Administration, outside the security scope; a check of their other callers is still to be done); the shadow comparison of Document, Controlled Copy and Copy Batch, the policy `actors` column and the six Document Revision Integrity flags (each needs its own owner decision, see the plan of 2026-11-20).
- **Checked.** Compile of main and test; the suites that touch the changed classes (route gate, policy, copy authorization, document authorization, profile, adapters, registry) green; on DEV after rebuild: V830 recorded, the six permissions gone, no permission set item points at a missing permission, the two removed routes answer 404, every screen read of the security area answers 200, and the earlier live scripts still pass (department level 21/21, copies 36/36, Object Rule removal 13/13).

## NOTIFICATION-PRIORITY-AND-INBOX-2026-11-20 — priority of a notification is configurable (Low, Medium, High) and filterable; the inbox is redesigned (option A)

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Mức độ quan trọng của thông báo sẽ tính dựa trên Detail [cột Details của Notifications Administration]. Thiết kế chức năng cấu hình cho thông báo email/in-app có select chọn mức độ Low, Medium hoặc High. Thêm filter lọc." and "chọn phương án A" for the inbox.
- **One priority per notification, not per channel.** The priority belongs to the notification (the event), and both its in-app and its e-mail screen edit the same value. The in-app notification a person receives gets that priority when it is created. Reading it as one value per channel (an e-mail importance of its own) was not asked for and is not built.
- **Catalog stays the default.** `notification_event_definitions.priority` is the default; `notification_policies.priority` (V831, nullable, CHECK LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH) holds an administrator's choice. Choosing the default stores NULL, so a notification can be put back to it. Effective priority = policy value, else catalog value (`NotificationPolicy.effectivePriority`), used by the list, the detail and the dispatcher.
- **CRITICAL is not selectable.** The 2 events the catalog marks critical keep it (the select then also offers Critical); no other notification can be given it (400 "Invalid priority"). The owner listed Low, Medium and High only.
- **Audit and concurrency.** A change is an `updatePolicy` change like the others: optimistic lock (`expectedUpdatedAt`), no electronic signature (wording-class change), audit field "Priority" with the value before and after. A change applies to notifications created from now on; notifications already in a person's inbox keep the priority they were sent with.
- **Filters.** Notifications Administration: Priority filter (server-side, `priority` on `GET /notification-administration`). Inbox: the server takes several priorities separated by commas (`critical,high`) for the "Critical / High" chip.
- **Inbox (option A).** Grouped by day (Today, Yesterday, Earlier), the whole line opens the record (it could not be opened from the list before), unread marked by bold text, a bar and a dot, priority badge only for Critical and High, chips Unread / Critical-High / Document Control / Training Management / This week, Filters in the shared drawer, a load error is shown as an error with Try again instead of an empty "all caught up". Time stays `dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss`. Column sorting of the old table is gone (the list is newest first).
- **Not done.** Not browser-verified on DEV (no test password); the priority of an e-mail message that is not an event is not shown (it has none); no e-mail importance header; no e-signature for the priority change (confirm if QA wants one).

## SECURITY-AUTHORIZATION-SHADOW-REMOVAL-2026-11-20 — the engine decides alone for every step; the shadow comparison, its table and the cutover flags are removed

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20): "do all three open decisions as proposed".** This entry is the first: cutover of Document, Controlled Copy and Copy Batch from the shadow, and removal of the shadow code. The other two (the policy `actors`, the six Document Revision Integrity flags) are in the following entry.
- **What the source showed (it corrects what the plan documents said).** Document, Controlled Copy and Copy Batch were not in the shadow path any more: `DocumentMasterWorkflowAuthorizationService` (CANCEL, OBSOLETE) and `ControlledCopyAuthorizationService` (every action except REQUEST_COPY, which has no record yet and keeps its own evaluation) already ask the engine and nothing else, fail-closed, and the DEV environment had the cutover flag on for them. Every call site of the gate (`ShadowGuard`, `TrainingShadow`) passed a type of the set decided by the engine alone, so the shadow branch of the gate was never reached. The only step that still compared the engine with a permission check was `UPDATE_METADATA` of a Document (next-revision configuration).
- **Built.** `UPDATE_METADATA` is decided by the engine alone and fails closed (before: the engine when the flag was on, otherwise the permission `documents.document.configure_next_metadata`; the engine's answer was checked on DEV against holding that permission for four accounts). The gates `ShadowGuard` and `TrainingShadow` only ask the engine (no engine, an error or a refusal stop the step; a record that does not exist is left to the service). Deleted: `AuthorizationShadowEvaluationService`, `AuthorizationCutoverFlags`, the property `app.authorization.hybrid-engine.enabled-resource-types` and the environment variable of the compose file; V832 drops the table `authorization_shadow_evaluation_events`. A V831 of another piece of work (`notification_policy_priority`) exists; this one is V832.
- **Kept, with the reason.** The two class names `ShadowGuard` / `TrainingShadow` and the unused predicate/boolean argument of their `around` and `run` (about 90 call sites in 17 services): renaming and removing them changes no decision and is a separate mechanical step. REQUEST_COPY keeps `evaluateInternal` (explicit exception since the plan of 2026-11-15). The set `ENGINE_DECIDES` stays because the policy editor reads it to know that a policy of the type is decided by its relations.
- **Checked.** Compile; 949 unit and slice tests run, the 33 errors are Spring integration tests that cannot start without the notification encryption key (they fail the same way without this change); on DEV after rebuild: V832 recorded, the table is gone, the engine's answer for `UPDATE_METADATA` equals the permission for four accounts, and the earlier live scripts still pass (department level 21/21, copies 36/36, Object Rule removal 13/13).
- **Not proven.** No observation window with real traffic after this change; the earlier 348,491 comparisons (none with a different allow/deny) are DEV data, now deleted.

## POLICY-ACTORS-KEPT-ONLY-FOR-REQUEST-COPY-AND-SIX-FLAGS-KEPT-2026-11-20 — the second and third of the three open decisions

- **Decision 2 (owner, 2026-11-20, chosen among three options): keep the actors only for REQUEST_COPY.** The engine never reads a policy's actors. The one step that still reads them is REQUEST_COPY of a Controlled Copy (`ControlledCopyAuthorizationService#evaluateInternal`): it has no record yet, so it cannot go through the engine. The policy screen nevertheless asked for actors on every policy, which decided nothing. Built: V833 deletes the actors of every policy except REQUEST_COPY of a Controlled Copy (146 rows on DEV, kept as a record in `docs/validation/evidence/visibility_tiers_2026_11_20/removed_policy_actors_DEV_2026_11_20.csv`; the 2 rows of REQUEST_COPY stay); the request records no longer require actors; the service neither validates, stores, diffs nor restores actors for any other policy (`WorkflowActionPolicyService.usesActors`) and the preview no longer reads a missing list; the policy editor shows the Actors list, the actor templates and the actor words in the sentence only for REQUEST_COPY, and sends no actors otherwise; Document, Controlled Copy and Copy Batch policies (except REQUEST_COPY) are now flagged `ENGINE_DECIDES_ALONE` like the Revision ones, so the screens warn that a change takes effect at once. No decision changes (the engine did not read the removed rows). Tests rewritten to the new rule (a Revision policy accepts any actors and stores none; REQUEST_COPY keeps its actors on reset).
- **Decision 3 (owner, 2026-11-20, chosen among three options): keep the six Document Revision Integrity flags as they are.** The six flags are checked when a Reviewer or an Approver is CHOSEN (three services, about 30 places) while the rules of Separation Rules are checked when the button is pressed, and "different department" has no relation in the engine; the two kinds are not equivalent, so folding them would change when a violation is found. Nothing was changed. The screen is already one place: the flags are edited in Security & Authorization > Segregation of Duties > Document with the same checkbox list and one electronic signature.
- **Checked.** Compile; the policy, copy authorization and route-gate suites green; on DEV after rebuild: V833 recorded, no policy other than REQUEST_COPY carries actors in the database or in the API, a Revision policy can be previewed without actors and the preview says the policy is the only decision, REQUEST_COPY still lists its actors; the earlier live scripts still pass (department level 21/21, copies 36/36, Object Rule removal 13/13, engine UPDATE_METADATA 12/12).
- **Not done.** The REQUEST_COPY step itself was not exercised with a real request by two people; its code path is unchanged and its actors are still there. The sentence of the other three policy layouts (Matrix, Templates) was checked by unit tests and the Sentence layout by screen.

## NOTIFICATION-PRIORITY-COLOURS-2026-11-20 — the colour of a priority and of the in-app icon follows the priority; the icon colour is no longer chosen

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Low là xanh, Medium là vàng, High là đỏ. Thông báo in-app sẽ căn cứ vào đó để đổi màu cho khớp; Notification Icon phải bỏ chức năng đổi màu icon, màu icon cũng căn cứ theo Priority."
- **Colours.** Low green (emerald), Medium yellow (amber), High red. Critical, which an administrator cannot choose, is drawn red like High (no fourth colour was asked for).
- **Where it applies.** The priority badge (administration list and edit screen), the icon tile of an in-app notification in the inbox, the bell menu, the toast of a new notification and the toast sample of Edit Notification. The preview in Edit Notification changes as soon as the priority is changed, before Publish.
- **Icon colour removed.** The Colour picker and "icon colour" are gone from Notification Icon (the icon itself is still chosen). `notification_policies.icon_tone` is dropped (V832); a colour an administrator had chosen is lost and every notification now takes the colour of its priority. API: `PUT /notification-policies/{code}/appearance` takes `icon` and `expectedUpdatedAt` only; `iconTone` is no longer sent or read.
- **Not done.** Not browser-verified on DEV; the colour of an old notification follows its stored priority (it is not recomputed from the current setting).

## NOTIFICATION-INBOX-LAYOUT-2026-11-20 — the Notifications page has two layouts, chosen by the administrator in Configuration

- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-20).** "Tôi muốn phương án E và F. Admin có thể bật trong cấu hình Configuration." The owner did not say where; placed in **Configuration, General, Module Display, Notifications, Inbox layout**, next to the other per-module layouts (Training Materials, Knowledge Base, Audit Trail tabs). Recommendation accepted by following the owner's wish to have it in Configuration; not a separate owner choice of place.
- **Values.** `appearance.notificationInboxLayout` = `TABS` (option F: tabs All, High, Medium, Low, Unread with counts, plain list; the scope For Me / Systems moves into the Filters drawer) or `TIMELINE` (option E: scope tabs, days on a timeline with a dot coloured by priority, a column of filters from xl). Default `TABS`. Unknown values are refused on save (400) and read as `TABS`. Applies to every user after save; the bell menu and the toasts are unchanged. No migration: it lives in the general configuration, so it is audited like the other appearance settings.
- **Counts.** `GET /notifications/summary` gains `byPriority` (low, medium, high, critical, read ones included) for the tab counts. The High tab is Critical plus High (both red) and asks the server for `priority=critical,high`.
- **What the old table lost.** Sorting by column is gone from the Notifications page in both layouts (newest first); the No. and Action columns are gone. Open, Mark as read, Delete, Mark all, Clear read, Export and the filters stay.
- **Defect found and fixed.** The page's filter state started from defaults and was written to the address by one effect and read back by another, so a page opened with a filter in the address (for example `?priority=critical`) could loop forever. The state now starts from the address.
- **Not done.** Not browser-verified on DEV (no test password); the choice has no per-user override; the screens of the two layouts were wireframes (option E and F of the six) and the built page was not screenshot.
